Per chat, the journal holds the `seq` counter, the chain snapshot, pause entries, tombstones and held writes. Switches and user-message edits write nothing per record; an assistant edit writes its record's `accepted` (or a bare record).
- **Record size:**
  - compact encoding; per-turn targets of about 2 KB (Grounded) and 6 KB (Simulation); hard cap 32 KB;
  - **overflow** first coarsens into period-close events, then writes continuation chunks in the same namespace; **never truncate**;
  - materialization is capped per write;
  - **timelapse and catch-up** record derivable events as per-period aggregates and period closes, never raw. They stay concrete deltas, so the fold is unchanged (L21, L29). A period close moves everyone once, which would lose or invent what the POV saw on the way (`space.seen`, §7), so a coarse record ends with one `engine.observed` event carrying the difference. Grid positions (`space.pos`) close the same way, one per entity from where it stood first to where it stands last. A dense synthetic 600-agent-day segment produced 57k events when measured: 2.0 MB raw and 155 KB even after coarsening. Time was never the binding limit (38 ms per segment on a child process); the record size and the 5,000-event unit cap (§9.2) are.

## 5. Turn pipeline

### 5.1 Hooks and lifecycle

1. **`GENERATION_STARTED`.**
   - Journal a `pending` entry `{generationId, kind, messageId, swipeId}` from the event's `targetMessageId` and `targetSwipeId`. The event gives the target swipe for every type; for Continue its target message is null (G1), so the head swipe's content is captured instead (length and hash). A swipe or regenerate the player asked to Re-narrate (the **Swipe as…** chip, or every swipe when Settings makes Re-narrate the swipe default; Re-run is the default) names its source: the nearest earlier swipe of the target that holds a live record.
   - Open the turn's in-flight window (P2, §4.3).

2. **The guard** (the one context handler, `backend/ledger-io.ts`). Registered `required` where Lumiverse supports it (`required-context-handlers-v1`), so a guard that fails or isn't answered in time stops the generation rather than letting it go out without the engine. Lumiverse waits for it as long as the user's **Generation guard** timeout says (Settings → Timeouts), within its own 1–120 s; one registration serves every user, at the longest any of them set, the full 120 s before any user's settings are read. It is registered again only when that value changes, and never while a generation or a call of the guard is under way, since the host replaces it inside the handler list its chain walks. The guard only looks, in memory:
   - **A dry run, a type the engine doesn't play (Quiet), a chat without a world:** they go on as they are. A dry run changes nothing: no stop, no engine run, no entry filled, and no turn touched. What an earlier turn left in the world's book (a restart, a clear the host refused) is emptied first, so a prompt preview holds none of it; only while a reply's guard holds the book (§6.4), from writing that reply's sections until its prompt is assembled, does a preview show them.
   - **Impersonate** goes on from a book that holds no engine section, so no mind or secret and nothing of another turn reaches the player's own message: it takes the world's book for its prompt (§6.4), waiting for a reply's guard writing its sections and for that reply's prompt, and for sections being emptied, and empties whatever an earlier turn left there. A turn prepared for a reply holds nothing in the book, so it stays prepared. Impersonate gives the book back once its prompt is assembled (`GENERATION_IN_PROGRESS`) or it ends.
   - **Any other generation** goes on only when the engine prepared it: a prepared turn of its type (a swipe's is a regenerate's), on the same target, that no generation took yet, made from what the chat is now. **Freshness** is the chain stamp (the chat's messages as their events showed them; a new reply's trailing empty staged message doesn't count), the journal's epoch (bumped by every write to the journal outside a turn), and the other inputs (`TurnHooks.inputs`): the chat's world revision, the settings the turn reads and the chat's Outcome notes (§9.5: a decline reworded or dismissed on its chip). The settings are one hash of exactly what the pre phase, the plan phase and the sections read (`turnSettingsKey`): the helper model, Model use (each step, the phase budgets, the section budget), the minds settings, whether JSON blocks are hidden, and the engine run's own clocks (Engine run, its lore lookups, the time skip plan). Saving any other setting (a new chat's realism, time or check rules, the swipe default, what reading the reply applies on its own, Build's jobs, the other timeouts) keeps a prepared turn. Any change drops a prepared turn at once; age alone never does, so there is no time expiry. A dropped turn is never silent: its run ends failed, saying which of them changed (a message of the chat, a write to the journal, the world revision, the settings, the Outcome notes), and the chat's last message gets the flag "The reply didn't start, so nothing was generated" with that reason, **Retry** (the engine runs again, then the reply) and **Diagnostics**. No preset is among the inputs, and no preset save or settings write drops a turn: Lumiverse's pages save every setting in batches, and Workshop and other extensions save presets of their own. The one preset that counts is checked as the reply goes on.
   - **Going on,** the guard takes the world's book for the reply (§6.4), asks the host again which preset this generation is assembled with and which of its blocks its profile switches (§6.8), and writes the turn's sections. When what placed them changed since the engine ran (that preset's `lre_` outlets, guidance blocks or narrator rules, or the world's section placements), it writes nothing: the turn is dropped, the generation stopped, and the engine runs again for it (step 3), so a reply always goes out with sections placed by the preset it is assembled with. A write the host refuses drops the turn and stops the generation with the flag of a failed engine run (step 3). The guard consumes the turn as it lets the generation through: no later generation takes it.
   - **Sections that can't be emptied** stop the generation instead of going out with them: when the host refuses to empty what an earlier turn left in the book, an Impersonate or a dry run is stopped, and the chat's flag says "An earlier turn's sections couldn't be cleared, so nothing was generated", with why and Diagnostics; pressing the same button again tries again. A reply's guard writes over them as it writes its own turn's sections (§6.4).
   - **Otherwise** it answers `cancelGeneration`: Lumiverse stops the generation before its prompt (a new reply's staged message is deleted; a Regenerate's or swipe's blank swipe stays, and the engine run removes it), and the stop starts the engine run (step 3).
   - **A Lumiverse that can't stop a generation** (no `preAssemblyGenerationContext` contract) lets it go on: diagnostics record why, and Settings, Diagnostics and the chat say "This Lumiverse can't stop generation; engine state may be missing." When Lumiverse stops waiting for a required guard, the generation ends with an error and nothing is generated.

3. **The engine run**, at the stopped generation's `GENERATION_STOPPED`, on the chat's queue after whatever is ahead of it (a previous turn's post steps included), within the user's **Engine run** timeout: a limit that fires stops the run.
   - The turn is prepared as a `pending` entry of the stopped generation's type and target. It stays in memory, out of the journal, until a generation takes it, so a restart drops it and the next generation runs the engine again.
   - **A reply for an earlier message is refused** before any model call, until G11: Lumiverse's Regenerate and Continue act only on the last reply, so the restart can't make a swipe of an earlier one (an Edit and Send in place on an earlier message, or a swipe or Regenerate with an explicit `message_id` naming one). Its blank swipe is removed, nothing is generated, and the chat's flag says why and what to do instead, with no Retry: use Edit and Send as a branch, or regenerate the last reply. On a host that can't stop generations, such a reply goes through unplanned and is read as one (§4.3).
   - **Continue:** no plan. A Continue's target is known only at `GENERATION_ENDED` (G1); its sections are the head's, and the restart is Lumiverse's Continue, which continues the last reply.
   - **Re-narrate** (P3, §4.3): no model steps and no new plan. The turn copies its source's seed, `pre` events and `markersApplied`, re-validates the source's live ✎ fixes and materializations against the new swipe's fold prefix, and its narration renders from that prefix, with the source turn's Outcome, Montage and Outline lines, kept (in `plans.json`) when that turn's reply took them: a run that was dropped or failed never speaks for a seed it shares (`same_roll`). Its post steps run at `GENERATION_ENDED` and commit as a `narr` entry. When the source can't be reproduced (its record is gone, or a `pre` event no longer validates), the turn is planned as a Re-run with a fresh seed but still runs no model steps: the plan reads only what the turn bridge keeps for the turn, and there is no brief or prose outline. With no source at all (no earlier swipe holds a live record) it is a Re-run in full.
   - **Otherwise,** in this order:
     1. **Steps before the plan** (§5.2, §5.3), on the engine run's budget: the turn's intent (a palette or chip action recorded on its user message, else `interpret` when its signals fire; a typed duration longer than the user lets a skip run without asking (a day by default, §9.2) becomes a Timelapse offer rather than the turn's action, while a typed *until*, a part of the day or the next shift or commitment, runs however far off it is), side by side with `npc.choose` for the agents with the POV in the tie zone or on an invitation, while a call stays free for the judge; then `puzzle.answer` when an Answer needs judging, `judge` when the player described their own action and its check is one the adjudication policy would roll with the player's words counting, or leaves to the story (§11), and any agent choice still waiting; the minds (§24) beside them, and a timelapse's time skip with its beats' minds. The turn bridge keeps their answers in memory for the turn: its text and the path it is planned at (the chain before its reply, its user messages included), and the plan reads them there only at that path. A Re-run of the turn, a Retry of it, or the Regenerate that answers a held claim or check, while they are kept, reuses the intent, a puzzle answer's probability and the judgement without asking again, but asks `npc.choose` afresh and plans with its new decisions. A later turn with the same words, or this turn once an earlier message has changed under it, is read again from where the story is then: a reading never outlives the state it was made from.
     2. **The plan** (deterministic): fold to the parent with the target message's live markers applied (the target is the last reply: one for an earlier message is refused, above), and before the chat's first planned record its starting markers; then resolve the action with any judgement, a fight's round around it, the turn's **elapsed time** (§9.2), and simulation **in process** (D9): calendar processes, the day planner, LOD0, schedules, tracks and rules, and catch-up for up to four off-screen agents, bounded by deterministic units (days, agents, events), with any remainder left on the deterministic **backlog** (a timelapse runs whole inside its unit cap instead); then beats and the turn's chips. The plan goes on the `pending` entry with the chain it was folded on, the pinned hashes of the turn's user messages (P4), its seed and its events, and the turn bridge keeps its chips and section lines by seed (`plans.json` keeps the lines too), so a Re-run's plan never replaces another swipe's.
     3. **Steps after the plan:** `brief` and the prose `outline`, side by side on what is left of the budget. Their text goes to the Outline slot.
     4. **The sections** (§6): the run ensures the world's book for the chat's world revision, holding it while it does (a reply's guard writing it and that reply's prompt, an Impersonate's prompt and sections being emptied are waited for, never written over), renders the sections on the section budget (§6.2) from this run's own plan and prose (never another run's of the same seed) and places each in its section's entry or its preset outlet's twin (§6.8), by the preset the host names for the chat. It writes none of them: the reply's guard does as the reply goes on, so a turn waiting for its reply holds nothing in the book that another generation could assemble with. A planned turn's run goes into the inspector history (§6.5).
     5. No message writes besides removing a stopped Regenerate's blank swipe.
   - **Every enabled step that fails stops the run;** a step that is switched off doesn't run and isn't a failure. A failure is a model call that failed, came back empty or was cut off (a Decision call the helper answered after, and a lore lookup a later call of its step answered after, excepted), a mind that didn't answer, a time skip that didn't run, the plan, writing the sections, or the run's time limit. A call that fails transiently (a dropped connection, a timeout, a 429, a 5xx) is made again first, twice at most, 1 s and then 2 s later, inside the run's time; an empty or cut-off answer is never asked again (raise the step's Max tokens). A failed run generates nothing: its entries are emptied, diagnostics record the step and why, and the chat's last message gets a flag, "The engine didn't finish, so nothing was generated", saying what failed and why ("Priya Nair's mind: connection reset (after 2 retries)"; someone the player doesn't know is "a mind"), with **Retry** (Regenerate: the engine runs again, then the reply) and **Diagnostics**. A Retry asks only what failed while what the rest was made from is unchanged: the turn bridge keeps the reading, and the minds that answered are kept while the chain, the journal, the world revision, the player's message, the minds settings, the mind step and the preset's mind block are the same.
   - **Cancel.** The activity line's Cancel (`lre.cancel {chatId}`, the chat's own user only) aborts the chat's engine run, or the post phase reading a saved reply, through one per-chat abort signal that every model call of the run, its lookups and its minds take; a run between steps stops at the next one, the wait for another chat's book and the making of the sections included. A cancel is its own end, not a failure: the run generates nothing and asks no restart, no flag or `engine_failed` chip is shown, and diagnostics record the turn as skipped ("you cancelled the turn"). The player's message stays, so Regenerate (or Send) runs the turn again. A cancelled reply read commits as stopped, as a reply whose generation failed does: nothing its calls had found applies, no memory step runs, and the reply stands unread with **Retry read**. Once the run has prepared its turn there is nothing to cancel (Lumiverse's Stop stops the reply), and Cancel answers `{cancelled: false}`.
   - **Preview gates** (Settings → Steps and connections → Preview before playing, `gates`, each off by default). **The intent gate:** once a typed message's action is read (never a palette or chip action, which the player picked), the run stops there and waits, nothing generated, on an `intent_gate` chip on the player's message: the action, and its picks in words as the palette names them (`actionWords`: "Bluebird Coffee, with Danny Okafor"; a place or person by name only where the player character may know of it, a table row, a stat or a quest by the label the palette and the quest log give it, and text written as an id that names nothing left out: never an id, §7), with Play, Edit and Cancel. Edit, offered when the Actions palette has the action here, opens the palette at that action alone with the read's picks made (only those the palette could offer travel to the page); its Play sends them as the chip's `{params}`. The server keeps only the action's own params and checks them as the read is checked (`unfitRef`), then holds every person, place or thing the edit changed to the palette's own candidates (`paramCandidates`, and `companionCandidates` for who comes along), so a hand-made request can't aim it where the palette never would; a refusal is in the read's words ("it names no place you know") and the chip stays. A claim's param (§9.4) left as read stays the claim's, which makes the thing real; one the edit changed is checked like any other pick, and a real pick for it wins: the claim goes and nothing is made. `lre.action` checks its params the same way, so no request plays a guessed id of a hidden thing. `npc.choose` waits too, so a cancelled read spends no call on it. **The outline gate:** once the prose steps have run, the run stops and waits on an `outline_gate` chip: the Outline slot's lines (the brief, the engine's beats and the outline step's), with Play, Edit (the lines, one each) and Cancel. A line an unrevealed secret is in is never shown or edited; it stays with the outline as it was. Both end the run as an Ask first claim does (diagnostics: skipped, waiting on the player); Play and Edit keep the answer with the turn bridge, and the frontend's Regenerate resumes the turn. An intent let through plays as read or as edited, with no new `interpret` call. An outline let through plays the very plan it was shown for (the bridge keeps each turn's own plan with its path, so a new seed's rolls can't change what the player approved), asks no mind, judge, `npc.choose`, brief or outline again, and its reply is told the Outline as let through. It is for that one turn: once its generation takes the turn (`adopted`), the outline and plan go, so a Re-run swipe plans afresh and waits on the gate again. Cancel only takes the chip. A turn read again at another path (an earlier message changed) is gated again.
   - **The restart.** The frontend starts the generation again with Lumiverse's own command (`lre.generate`: Regenerate, or Continue), since an extension can start only a new message, never a Regenerate or a Continue; a new reply after a reply only the player can start again, so the chat says to press Send. **Exactly once:** only the page the stopped generation ran for (its `frontendSessionId`) is asked. With none named, the first page to open or resync the chat claims it; any other page's activity line says "Already started in another window", and the claim passes on only when its page disconnects (`FRONTEND_SESSION_CLOSED`) or says it no longer shows the chat, never on a timer. A page that never said what it shows (no engine frontend runs there, so nothing would start the reply) holds the claim only until a page that has said it shows the chat is there: such a page is asked instead, at the engine run or when it opens or resyncs the chat, so no page is left reading "Already started in another window" behind a page that can't start it. A page starts each prepared turn once, and the guard consumes it when it lets the generation through. While the asked page starts it, the activity line says "Waiting for the reply…". A page that slept through the ask is asked again when it reconnects, comes back into view or reopens the chat (hello, resync); while no page shows the chat, the line says "Ready; reopen the chat to continue". On a Lumiverse that names no page, every page is asked.
   - **Once the reply's prompt is assembled** (`GENERATION_IN_PROGRESS`), its entries are emptied and switched off and the book goes back; a reply that ends or stops before that empties them then. The turn's plan lines and prose become its seed's (what the reply's steps and a later Re-narrate read) when its reply takes it, never before.

4. **Narrator generation.**

5. **`GENERATION_ENDED`** (whenever content was saved).
   - **The saved reply** is `GENERATION_ENDED.content`, which equals `message.swipes[targetSwipeId]`. A normal turn saves with `MESSAGE_EDITED` (or `MESSAGE_SENT`, P4). Regenerate, swipe and both Edit-and-Send modes save with `MESSAGE_SWIPED` `updated`, whose `message.content` follows the *displayed* swipe when the user navigated mid-stream.
   - **An empty reply** (content `""` with no error; the measured model sometimes returned only reasoning) runs no post steps and commits the pre-reply events as `degraded`, with a **Retry** chip; the engine never regenerates on its own (D32).
   - **Post steps:**
     - extraction, including a bounded `time.advance` for narrated time beyond what this swipe has already applied, with that advance's catch-up, all or nothing (§9.2);
     - off-screen agents, in process (D9);
     - tracks and rules.
   - Validate with the bounds (§11), against the turn's fold prefix (P2, §4.3).
   - **Commit:** compute `path` over the chain stored with the `pending` entry plus the turn's user messages hashed per P4; compute `swipeHash`; write the record through the queue; clear the shadow `pending` entry; close the window (P2). The host keeps swipe navigation enabled during a generation. If an earlier message changed meanwhile, the new record is off-path: a swipe change makes it a rebased view (re-validated, with a Keep-report chip), and a user-message edit, including one to the turn's own user messages (P4), puts it under the pause; an assistant edit leaves it on-path (§4.3).
   - **Continue** (head target): `GENERATION_ENDED.content` holds the whole swipe, re-rendered. A head without a record (a greeting before the first turn) is accepted unread, like a non-head target. Otherwise extraction reads **only the suffix after the captured content**, and only if the content still starts with it. The host re-renders the whole combined swipe, and literal-brace shielding is keyed by the full content's hash, so a prefix with literal braces can change; then the Continue is accepted unread, as for a non-head target. The suffix is validated against the head's fold, and its events become a **`post` entry of kind `cont`** whose `seq` is assigned when the Continue commits. Everything journaled before the Continue started therefore folds before it, as it was in effect when the suffix was generated; writes made while it streamed are held by its window and fold after it (P2). `swipeHash` is updated; the head's own `path` chains only earlier messages and is unchanged. Effects already in the record are never re-read.
   - **Non-head guard.** `GENERATION_ENDED.messageId` names the continued message, and an explicit `message_id` can name any assistant message. If it is not the head assistant message, extraction is skipped and **no events are appended to any record**, since a non-head append would change every later path hash. This is an assistant edit (§4.3): the new content becomes the record's `accepted` identity (a bare record for a message without one), later paths are unchanged, and the message offers Re-read edit. Re-verification of a message edited during a Continue waits for its `GENERATION_ENDED` or `GENERATION_STOPPED` (step 6), because the save emits `MESSAGE_EDITED` first.
   - **Status:** `ok` when the turn was planned and its reply isn't empty; otherwise `degraded`. A turn without a plan still has its reply read, unless a pause sits before it. A pause that arrives while the reply streams (an earlier user message edited) also makes the record `degraded`: its plan stands, since it was made on its path, but the reply goes unread.
   - Unresolved confirm chips from the previous reply expire at this send; nothing is applied.
   - **Swipe index:** from the `GENERATION_STARTED` note, for every type. Both Edit-and-Send modes stage a blank swipe; in branch mode it is in the branch chat, where every event of the turn lands.

6. **`GENERATION_STOPPED`.**
   - A generation the guard stopped starts the engine run (step 3); nothing else happens for it.
   - If content was saved (other than by a Continue), commit the pre-reply events as `stopped` (Retry read / Discard); otherwise drop the plan. Either way the window closes (P2).
   - **Retry read of a stopped reply.** The reply was saved unread, so a Retry read reads everything saved by then, a Continue after it included, and replaces what was read meanwhile (that Continue's suffix read, the proposals confirmed from its chips, and that read's chips). It then marks the reply read, so no change or charge lands twice and no Retry read is left; a Re-read edit of a stopped reply marks it read too.
   - **A stopped Continue.** The host saves `original + postfix + partial` onto the continued swipe without a macro re-render, then emits `GENERATION_STOPPED` with only the partial suffix and no `messageId`. Any message may also be edited meanwhile, and `updateMessage` emits `MESSAGE_EDITED` for every write, metadata-only ones included. The target is therefore found by content: among the messages whose `MESSAGE_EDITED` arrived during the Continue, the one whose swipe `targetSwipeId` (from `GENERATION_STARTED`) now ends with the partial suffix; if several do, the one whose content before the Continue (`swipeHash`, `accepted` or the chain snapshot) is a prefix of it. Every other edit made during the Continue is classified normally (§4.3). The new content becomes the target's `accepted` identity (a bare record if it has none), as for any assistant edit. On a head whose record holds events the chip is **Retry read**, which extracts only the partial suffix into a `cont` entry that takes its `seq` then; `swipeHash` then covers the text and `accepted` is cleared. Elsewhere it is Re-read edit.

7. **Restart recovery.** An assistant message whose reply was saved but that has no record is committed from its shadow `pending` entry as `degraded`, unread (*Not fully simulated*); that closes any window left open, and its held writes are then journaled (P2). Only windows the journal held when the backend read it are recovered: a generation under way in this backend keeps its window and its `pending` entry however often a page opens or resyncs the chat, so its reply is still read and committed with its plan. A `pending` entry whose staged reply is gone (the host removes it at restart) is dropped: no record is made, its window closes and its held writes are journaled. Tombstoned (discarded) records are never restored; tombstones match by message id, `swipeHash` and stored-record hash (§4.3).

8. **Swipe options.**
   - **Re-run** (the default): a fresh seed (the same roll where the world's `seedMode` is `same_roll`). The turn keeps the reading kept for it (`interpret`'s answer, a puzzle answer's probability and any judgement) while the turn bridge holds it and the chain before the turn is unchanged, so the dice change; `npc.choose` is asked again, and so are the brief, the outline and the post steps. The source's ✎ fixes and GM edits are carried after the new turn's events (P3).
   - **Re-narrate:** P3 (§4.3): the same seed, `pre` events and `markersApplied`, the source's ✎ fixes and materializations re-validated against the new swipe's fold prefix, narration rendered from that prefix, and new post steps committed as a `narr` entry.

9. **Delivery** on `WORLD_INFO_ACTIVATED` for the generation the guard let through: a section whose entry activated is marked delivered (`verified`), one whose entry didn't is not (`unverified`: the host's World Info budget left it out, say), and the inspector record reads Activation confirmed once every entry the turn filled activated. Activation happens before the host resolves outlets, so it can't tell whether an assembled block holds a twin's outlet (§6.8).

10. **Queued writes.** UI edits, between-turn materialization (`post`), confirmations, markers, Keep and Discard are journaled at once outside a window and held inside one (P2); each takes its `seq` and `at` when it is journaled. Journaled writes, late commits and re-asserts are flushed per message through its queue once no generation is in flight.

### 5.2 Steps
- **Phases:**
  - `pre`: the engine run, before the reply.
  - `post`: `GENERATION_ENDED`.
- **Engine steps:** `time`, resolve, simulate, `plan.day`, `beats`.
- **No word list reads typed text.** `interpret` reads every typed message while its step is on (`typed_input`): whether a message holds an action, which ones and with what targets is its call. An earlier build gated it on a deterministic classifier (verbs, a known name as a target, quantities, money); real chats word actions in more ways than any list holds, so the classifier and its `classifier` signal are retired. Settings and world step templates saved with that signal read it as `typed_input`. A palette or chip action, or a reading kept for a Re-run of the same message, needs no call.
- **What `interpret` reads** (`backend/interpret-context.ts`), besides the typed message, which is its user message as typed:
  - **The catalog** of what the POV can do now: the world's actions and the built-in ones, each with its id, label, `description`, params and needs. A required ref lists the ids that may fill it, as the palette offers them (only those the POV may know); an optional one, which the palette never lists, takes an id of its kind named elsewhere; a place ref points at the places; a table param lists its rows' ids, Attempt's stat the stats and Buy's item what is sold here. Needs are each requirement that reads a param in the words of its shape (`needsOf`, sharing its shapes with a decline's reason), never the expression, which can name a secret. An action a level-of-detail or money-feature gate rules out, or with a required ref nothing the POV may know can fill, is left out; a requirement that reads no param is otherwise neither listed nor checked, so an action an earlier one in the message makes possible can still be queued. Letting time pass is always there, with its `span` (the input's own words for how long or until when) required, so a read that picks it always says how long (§9.2).
  - **The scene** as the POV knows it: the day and time and where it is (a place it can't see is somewhere it can't name), who is there, the people the typed message names who aren't, each with where and how long ago the POV last saw them (its own observations, `space.seen`, §7, never where they are now), what it carries, its vehicles, everyone and everything else the catalog lists, and the invitations waiting on its answer (`castlife.invitations`), each by name and id; what the last message left unplayed, the words of the Send chip on the reply before it (§11), read as the send that typed this message lapsed it, and not when this message is those very words; then the places it can see, nearest first.
  - **The messages before it**, newest back, at most the interpret card's **Recent messages** (`recent`, by default 8 messages and about 3,000 tokens; 0 reads none, and neither has a ceiling), each on a line, a reply as the engine reads it (§6.7); the oldest that doesn't fit whole keeps its end, where a reply leaves off. A reading that hangs on the reply before it ("I take what she offered") is resolved from them.
  - **Its lore**, as the step's lore policy gives it (§5.4).
  - **Its instructions:** read what the player character does, resolving what the input refers to from the scene and the messages; first person, a stage direction ("[Alex heads to the roof]") and third-person narration of the POV all count; speech alone, out-of-character text that doesn't say time passes, questions, requests, others' actions, hypotheticals, plans for another time, recaps and feelings are no action; only catalog actions, never an invented one; when the context still leaves the action or its target unclear, no action and a short `reason` (logged), never a guess; params as world ids. **A time skip is the timelapse, whatever else the input says:** an out-of-character instruction that time passes or skips ahead ("OOC: over the next hour, I sort the stock", "(OOC: skip ahead two hours)", "[OOC] later that evening"), and in character a span spent on something no catalog action covers ("I spend the next hour sorting the stock"). What it says happens in that time fills it: it is never an action of its own, queued, left in words or put after it as `then`, so an instruction to skip is never read as the activity it describes, at that action's own duration. Its params hold its `span` in the input's own words (§9.2). A move the input takes for granted counts: one that has the POV somewhere other than the scene's place (beside someone who isn't there, at a fixture of another place, as in "I sit down next to Marcus" while the POV is at the market) reads the move there first, as "I go there" would, to where that person was last seen or the place with that fixture; and when it takes as done what the last message left unplayed ("I set my chips on the counter" after the buy didn't play), those actions are read first, in order. The output contract is strict JSON, repaired once when it doesn't parse (§18.4). On the Decision route each choice's criterion is its label and description, and the decision's state carries the scene and the messages; "none" is no action, which an out-of-character instruction that time passes isn't.
- **Decision steps** (routing per D10: `interpret` on the helper; `npc.choose` on the Decision connection; on a decision error, the helper, then engine-only; no blind retry):
  - Built-in purposes: `interpret`, `npc.choose`, `contradiction`, **`puzzle.answer`** (an authored opt-in per puzzle) and **`judge`** (the player's described approach, rated 0 to the policy's top with a one-line reason, and under narrative authority each outcome's probability, the exact odds given as guidance; §11). On the Decision route `judge` asks a score question and a choice question, so it has no reason.
  - The first option is the safe default.
  - Answers are probabilities, sampled with a seeded RNG. A helper yes/no without a probability is no answer.
  - On the Decision route, `interpret` asks a choice question over the catalog (and "none"); when the chosen action takes parameters, a helper call fills them (a second call), because the Decisions API answers questions, not parameters. With no call left for it, the action goes unread, as when the call fails (§12), rather than playing as one that names nothing.
  - `interpret` returns the input's actions in order, at most five (`ACTION_QUEUE_LIMIT`, §11): the first as its action, and the further ones it reads as engine actions as its queue, each with its params and its words as the player would type them ("I walk to Bluebird Coffee and buy a latte" → the walk, then "I buy a latte"); what follows them that no catalog action reads stays in words (`next`), and so do actions past the bound or after one it can't read. It is told to read every action, not only the first, and to leave out an incidental one that fits no catalog action or names nothing listed, which never hides the actions after it. Time passing is never queued: it is `then`'s, and a timelapse given as the last action reads as `then`. A queued action is read as the first is (money in minor units, the player's way of paying, its ref params checked). One carrying an unrevealed secret is dropped on its own and what follows it is left in words; one that names nothing that fits is dropped on its own with its own decline chip ("Pay: couldn't"), as a first action is, and the rest still plays. What the turn doesn't play, the queued actions left when the queue stops and then `next`, is offered on one chip that sends it as the next message, so it plays as the next turn with its own prices, time and checks. Whether a piece is an action, and whether it only lets time pass (time after the actions is `then`'s, offered as a Timelapse), is the read's call, never a word list's ("grab some milk", "get a latte" and "pick up groceries" are offered as the read gives them), so a piece is kept off the chip only as junk: a placeholder for nothing ("none", "N/A"), or the message or its first action said again, compared as words without the player's "I", whose chip would play it twice. A piece is at most 120 characters (a longer one is dropped, never cut). One carrying an unrevealed secret (§7), or naming someone or something the POV can't see or what leads to a secret lore entry (its title, an alias, a key, a topic or a lookup term), is dropped on its own, and the actions still play. Time the message lets pass after its actions isn't offered while some of them are left. On the Decision route only the helper call that fills a chosen action's params can read further actions, and it is shown only the chosen action, so it queues only more of that action (a second Buy) and leaves the rest in words for the chip: the Decisions API answers one choice, and choosing each further action there would cost a choice and a params call apiece. A choice with no params, or no call left to fill them, has none.
  - `interpret`'s ref params must name the right real thing, as the POV knows it: an entity of the param's kind that the POV may know (itself, someone it has met, its own, or visible where it stands; the ids the catalog lists), or a place where it takes places. A read that doesn't (a name the story made up, as in "I pay Rena" with no Rena in the world, or "I pull on my work jacket" with no jacket) is dropped: an incidental action that names nothing never displaces the move the message is about, so the message's other actions still play ("I pull on my work jacket and walk down to Alder Automotive" walks there), and with none left the message plays as plain roleplay. Only a read can be dropped this way: an action that fails a requirement or a check when it plays still stops the queue (§11), since what follows may hang on it. A guessed id of something the POV can't know reads as one nothing has, so the drop never tells the player a hidden thing exists. Each dropped action's reply gets its own decline chip, "Pay: couldn't", about that action alone, saying why: what the param named, as the player typed it ("Rena isn't anyone in this world"), a real thing by its name when it is of another kind ("Bluebird Coffee isn't a person"), or that it has none. It repeats no name the player didn't type, none the POV may not know, none that leads to a secret lore entry (its title, an alias, a key, a topic or a lookup term), none carrying an unrevealed secret, and nothing too long for a name ("it names no one you know"). The Outcome reports what played. As for a refused change (§11), the next Outcome slot is told each dropped action didn't happen, and *Remove it* or *Accept* rewords that action's note. Both are about the player's message, not the reply, so a Continue, Re-read edit or Retry read of the reply keeps them.
  - **Player claims.** A ref param that takes claims (`claim: "held"` on Give, Use, Wear and Drop, `"here"` on Take) lets `interpret` read a thing nothing lists in the input's own words, with its sort (`thing`: food, drink, weapon, key, document, money or other). When the param names nothing that fits, the claim is judged before the drop. A thing the POV may know by those words (the same noun with every word typed; one it holds first, then one where it stands) is taken instead, and keeps its own rules; a place counts only by its whole name, and drops the action. Money (a cash phrase, or the sort `money`) is never made, in any mode, and its decline says so. The action's other params must fit. The chat's policy then decides (`claims`, layered as the realism levels are, §9.1: Story makes it real, Grounded makes it real with the cap, Simulation asks first). **Hold me to the world** drops it as above. **Make it real** plays it with the claim. **Ask first**, and Make it real for a key, a weapon or a document, or with the cap for a thing whose catalog price is over the world's claim cap (the economy pack's `claimCap`, else twice its `meals_out` typical price), waits: the engine run ends in its pre phase with nothing generated and no restart, the question chip ("Coffee isn't in this world yet. Add it and give it?") sits on the player's message with the read kept, and **Add** or **Not an action**, then the frontend's Regenerate, plays the message in one reply, with that action or without it. A played claim makes its thing just before its action (`gear.claim`): an item with `fields.claimed: true` and `notable: true` (so Gear 1 shows it), the POV's own and in its hands (lying where it stands, for Take), priced and sorted by its catalog row, weighed and slotted by a gear row it is like, its id from the turn's seed, its place in the queue and its name, never the name alone; a held thing of the same catalog row or name stands for it. Its making leads the action's events, so the rules see it. A claimed consumable is cleared (`gone`) once given to someone else or used, at any gear level, with its holder and owner kept, so what the gift did stands. The reply's "Added: a coffee" chip undoes it with what its action did. A hidden thing counts as absent, by its id or its name: in every mode a guess plays exactly as a made-up thing does (made as a new claimed thing, never linked to the hidden one, under Make it real; asked about under Ask first; dropped under Hold me to the world), so nothing the player sees differs from a world without it. A claim never duplicates a thing only where that costs no tell, which is never for a hidden one: secrecy wins over the duplicate. A requirement on an authored id (`carries(self, shop_office_key)`) never passes on a claimed thing.
  - **Tracking gaps.** The player character's own thing (`owner` the POV) that the world has neither on them nor where they stand is a gap in what the engine tracks, not a veto: whether `interpret` names it by id or by words ("I put on my clothes" while the world keeps them at home), a claim param's action never fails and stops the message for want of it, but goes to the chat's claims policy as a claim of that thing (`existing`). Make it real brings that very thing to their hands (or where they stand, for Take), never a second one; Ask first asks ("Clothes isn't with you as far as the world knows. Have it with you and wear it?"), as do keys, weapons and documents under Make it real; Hold me to the world drops the action with its decline, and the rest of the message still plays. It names only what the POV may know, so it tells nothing; someone else's thing elsewhere keeps its rules.
  - **Inferred checks.** While the chat's policy suggests or rolls (and allows a skill the sheet has), the catalog offers the built-in **Try something** (`try`) in place of the world's own skill actions, listing the skills the policy allows and the difficulty bands within its bounds, and the instructions read an attempt whose outcome is uncertain that no other catalog action covers as it, never beside or instead of an action that covers it; a reaction the input asks for counts, even phrased as a question ("I watch Marcus as he answers: is he lying?"). With the policy off, the catalog keeps the world's skill actions (Use a skill, Make a save, the Sheets pack's Attempt), so typed text reads them as it did before inferred checks ("I use my Mechanics skill on the engine" is Attempt or Use a skill, rolling that action's check), and offers no Try something (§11).
- **Prompted steps:**
  - **Whose prompt runs:** the user's own for the step (`StepConfig.template`, Settings → Steps and connections, `ownTemplate` in `runsOf`), else the world's templates for the purpose, else the built-in. The user's prompt runs as a world template of the step's own kind (`text` for `interpret`, the prose steps and `extract`; `decision` for `npc.choose`, `contradiction`, `puzzle.answer` and `judge`), so it has exactly that effect, and it takes the place of the world's templates except their `effects` ones, which are calls of their own. A blank prompt is none. The minds steps' own prompts are `minds.prompts` (Settings → Minds, the same editor in both pages), so `mind`, `memory` and `consolidate` keep no `template`; the `skip` step's `template` is the skip plan's (§9.4: its placeholders `{{world}}`, `{{pov}}`, `{{place}}` and `{{clock}}` the skip's start, then `SKIP_PLAN_ANSWER`, which no prompt replaces). A Model use preset keeps every `template`. The prose steps' built-ins are `PROSE_PROMPTS` (`shared/steps.ts`), shown in Settings to start from.
  - **Which placeholders each step gets** is one table, `STEP_VARS` (`shared/steps.ts`), with `VAR_TEXT` for what each holds; the vars panel beside every template editor (Settings → Steps and connections, Settings → Minds, Build's step templates) reads it, and lint's lists match it.
  - World step templates use the engine placeholders only: `{{world}}`, `{{pov}}`, `{{place}}`, `{{clock}}`, `{{scene}}`, `{{facts}}`, `{{present}}`, `{{outcome}}`, `{{beats}}`, `{{brief}}`, `{{user}}`, `{{reply}}`, `{{recent}}`, and what the player character has and knows (`slotVars`: each the non-hint lines its sections' renderers have now, as the narrator reads them, so only what the POV may know, and empty in a world without the section): `{{gear}}` (slot `gear`), `{{money}}` (`money`), `{{property}}` (`property`, `vehicles`), `{{positions}}` (`people`) and `{{knowledge}}` (`quest`); and two only some steps are given: `{{usual}}` (the usual routines of the people with the player character, `usualRoutineForExtract`) in the steps that read the reply (`extract`, `choices` and every `effects` template), and `{{sections}}` (the sections the narrator has this turn) in the `outline`. The prompted steps fill them all: `brief`, `outline`, `choices`, `extract` and every `effects` template. An `interpret` template on the helper route gets only `{{world}}` and `{{pov}}`, and the decision steps' templates (`npc.choose`, `puzzle.answer`, `judge`, a `decision` contradiction template, and `interpret` on the Decision route) are sent as written, with no placeholder filled. `{{brief}}` is the turn's brief once its step has run: an outline template naming it waits for the brief, the steps after the reply read the plan's (while the backend still holds the plan, as for `{{outcome}}`), and in the brief step itself it is empty. `{{recent}}` is the Journal's latest five lines before the turn, as the player sees them there (only what the POV could see, worded with labels); a ✎ fix or GM edit the player may see is a line of its own, so it takes one of the five. Host macros in a template are not evaluated, because helper calls are quiet generations that skip prompt assembly; the import lint lists any macro a template carries. Every placeholder is player-visible content, and every one goes through `safeVars`, which drops a line carrying an unrevealed secret. Lint warns of a placeholder its step isn't given (`unknown_placeholder`), a blank template outside the minds steps, which takes the place of the built-in instructions with none (`step_blank`), and a contradiction template whose output isn't `decision`, which takes the built-in check's place without being a check (`step_contradiction_output`).
  - Output `text` or `effects` (bounded like extraction; money amounts in an `effects` output are plain amounts in the world's currency as written, 8 for $8, and the engine converts them to minor units as it does for extraction). Built-in purposes: `brief`, `outline`, `extract`, `choices`.
- **Where each output goes:** `brief` leads the Outline slot as "Brief: …"; a prose `outline` adds Outline lines after the engine's beats; `contradiction` becomes a chip (Re-narrate / It's fine) plus a correction line in the next Outcome slot; `choices` become chips that send the suggestion as the player's message, and so does what the turn didn't play of `interpret`'s read (the queue's leftover actions and `next`); `effects` templates produce proposals that go through the extraction modes and bounds, after the reply only. Each brief, beat and suggestion is leak-checked whole, before any cut, and withheld when it carries an unrevealed secret anywhere in it; only then is a brief cut to 600 characters and a beat to 300, while a suggestion longer than 120 is dropped rather than cut. The narrator sees prose steps through the slots it already reads, and corrections and suggestions stay under the player's control.
- **Outline:** `beats` always renders the deterministic outline; prompted outline steps stack on top.
- **Signals:** `typed_input`, `reply`, `scene_change`, `slot_crossed`, `day_start`, `beat`, `agents_present`, `present`, `offscreen`, `tie_zone`, `invitation`, `timelapse`, `conflict`, `every_n:<k>`, `always`, `never`. The retired `classifier` reads as `typed_input`. A world step template for the retired `select` purpose is dropped when its world loads.
- **Secrecy batching rule:** questions that carry secrets never share a call with prose-producing steps.

### 5.3 Model-use presets and budgets (D3, D13, D25)

| Preset | Pre-reply (engine run) | Post-reply | Model steps a turn: most | Engine budget (sections) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Lean** (floor) | `interpret` on every typed message; `puzzle.answer` when an Answer needs judging | `extract` | narrator + up to 2 | 2,000 tokens |
| **Standard** (default) | `interpret` on every typed message; `judge` when the adjudication policy needs it; `npc.choose` on `tie_zone`/`invitation`; `puzzle.answer` when an Answer needs judging | `extract`; off-screen `npc.choose` on `tie_zone` | narrator + up to 4 | 4,000 tokens |
| **Full** | `interpret` on every typed message (a palette or chip action, or a reading kept for a Re-run, needs no call), `npc.choose` for all present agents, `judge` when the policy needs it, `puzzle.answer` when an Answer needs judging; then `brief` and the prose `outline` | `extract`, off-screen `npc.choose`, `contradiction` | narrator + up to 8 | 8,000 tokens |
| **Custom** | Any step, signal, provider or model | Same | Depends on the steps enabled; the call meter shows it | User budgets |

**Measured calls per turn** (the test suite's busiest case: one typed turn that fires every signal, with an open tie for an agent with the POV and one elsewhere, and a reply; the narrator is not counted):

| Preset | Calls | Which |
|---|---|---|
| Lean | 2 | `interpret`, `extract` |
| Standard | 4 | `interpret`, `npc.choose` ×2 (with the POV before the plan, off-screen after the reply), `extract` |
| Full | 7 | Standard + `brief`, prose `outline`, `contradiction` (an eighth call judges a puzzle answer or the player's approach when one needs it; an Answer has no check, so never both) |
| Custom, every step on `always` | 8 | Full + `choices` (`puzzle.answer` runs only when an Answer needs judging) |

The most a turn can run is what the preset's steps and phase budgets allow: each step takes one call from its phase's budget when it starts. Full's 8 is the 7 of the busiest tested turn plus a judged puzzle answer or approach. On Standard the judge's call is held ahead of `npc.choose`'s, so a judged turn whose action `interpret` read spends both calls and leaves the choice of an agent with the POV to the numbers; when `interpret` spends none (a palette or chip action with words of the player's own, or a reading kept for a Re-run), both run. Lean never judges an approach, and `puzzle.answer` is on in every preset until turned off. No typical count is given: the earlier figures were measured with the retired lore-selection step and haven't been measured again. The call meter shows each turn's real count. A step can cost more than its one call: each lore lookup round takes one from its phase's lookup budget (§5.4), and a JSON repair, a Decision-to-helper fallback or `interpret`'s one retry after a transient failure adds a call that no budget is charged for.

**Budgets**
- Budgets are a **call count per phase for model steps**; how long each phase may take is a timeout (below). Simulation is bounded only by deterministic units.
- Defaults, as calls before / after the reply: Lean 1 / 1; Standard 2 / 2; Full 8 / 4. Call counts, lookup counts and every token budget have no ceiling, only a floor where zero would break the call (1 max token).
- **Lore lookups** have their own budget per phase (`PhaseBudget.lookup`: rounds), apart from the calls: Lean none; Standard 4 / 3; Full 6 / 4 (§5.4); the window from the phase's start in which one may begin is a timeout. The calls counted above leave them out; the call meter counts them.
- Before the reply, the turn's intent and the choices of agents with the POV run side by side (the choices only while a call stays free for the judge), then `puzzle.answer`, `judge` and any choice still waiting; after the plan, `brief` and `outline` side by side, except an outline template naming `{{brief}}`, which runs after the brief. After the reply, independent steps run side by side.
- When a phase's calls or time run out, each remaining step uses its fallback: utility-only NPC choice, beats-only outline, a check rolled as if unjudged (one the story would decide goes to the dice), or plain roleplay plus extraction. The post phase keeps the user's deadline, with no hidden floor: a budget of 0 ms means no post call.
- **Timeouts.** Every wall clock is the user's (`UserSettings.timeouts`, Settings → Timeouts, `shared/timeouts.ts`), in ms or `"none"`, with no ceiling and a floor only where zero breaks the wait; no preset sets one. Defaults: engine run 5 min, lookups 2 min, reading the reply 5 min, guard 2 min, tab requests 5 min, preset check 1 min, minds run 10 min, Import/Refine call 10 min, token count 30 s, lane start-up and heartbeat 1 min, MCP server reconnect 1 min (§23). A limit past a timer's range (about 24.8 days) runs as none. One that runs out aborts with a `TimeoutFired` naming its setting, which the diagnostics record with how to raise it; the engine run's stops the run (§5.1). A call that fails transiently is made again, twice at most, 1 s and then 2 s later, inside its step's time; those pauses aren't timeouts. Lumiverse's own limits are one table (`HOST_LIMITS`): context handler 1–120 s, decisions 30 s per call, background process start-up 1–120 s and heartbeat 0–120 s, shared-RPC reads 10 s, idle event socket 120 s; a setting that feeds one is asked of Lumiverse within it. An older settings file's phase wall clocks and lookup windows move into `timeouts`, each still at an old preset default (or the old cap) rising to the new default.
- **Reasoning models:** a reasoning model's thinking counts toward `max_tokens`; at 160 helper calls failed, and at 2,000 a live DeepSeek V4 Flash call on a long chat spent it all thinking and answered nothing. The helper model's max tokens defaults to 8,192 (`HELPER_MAX_TOKENS`; at least 1, no ceiling, and the cards warn below 4,096), preset steps set none of their own, Import and Refine calls take at least 16,384 (`PROPOSAL_MAX_TOKENS`), and lore tokens default to 800 (Lean), 1,600 (Standard) or 2,400 (Full) per step.
- **The helper model** (`UserSettings.helper`): the connection, model, reasoning effort, max tokens and sampling parameters (`temperature`, `top_p`, `top_k`, `frequency_penalty`, `presence_penalty`, by the host's `GenerationParameters` names) that every call through `Steps` runs with unless its step sets its own (`helperCallOf`): a step with its own connection runs on it with its own model, else on the helper model's connection and model; its effort, max tokens and each parameter are its own, else the helper model's. A null connection is Lumiverse's default connection (`generate.quiet` without `connection_id`), with that connection's model. The model goes as `parameters.model`, the host's per-request override; the parameters go in `parameters`, which the host merges over the connection's preset, so one left out stays the connection's own; the provider decides which it honours. The effort reaches the host as `generate.quiet`'s `reasoning` override: `inherit` sends none (the connection's reasoning binding, else the user's setting), `off` sends `{source: "off"}`, any other `{source: "custom", apiReasoning: true, effort}`. The Decisions API (`decisions.evaluate`) takes only a connection and a model, so Decision steps run with their connection's own settings; their lookup pre-pass and their helper fallback run on the helper model. Presets never change the helper model.
- The call meter shows each turn's calls, tokens, latency and lore lookups in the HUD, with averages and the 95th percentile in Settings. It is kept per user in `userStorage/call-meter.json` (the last 200 turns and the current one), so a restart keeps its numbers.
- **Lean** is the documented floor of D3 and does not meet "a model decides" on its own.

### 5.4 Lore for the engine's helpers

Pinned lore (§24.5) comes first: every call of a helper step reads what is pinned to that step, whatever its lore policy (the user chose it for the step), then, unless its policy is `none`, what is pinned where the POV is and to the places holding it, never a character's pins, given ahead of the prefetch and searched with what is left of the step's lore tokens. The step's lore trace marks those entries `pinned`, and a note says when pinned text went over the step's lore tokens.

Every model step reads the world lore it needs (§4.2) through the engine's own search; native World Info activation plays no part. The lore it searches is the world's own and the entries of the World Info books the chat reads, read live once per engine run (§4.2 World Info as lore), under the same visibility rules.

- **The search** (`engine/lore.ts`, pure): `loreSearch({query?, terms?, links?, topics?, purpose, limit?, exact?, tokenBudget})`, `loreGet(id)` and `loreList(topic)`. It reads the folded state and the world only and never calls a model. With no `limit` no count of hits cuts it: a prefetch and a lookup give every entry they find within the step's lore tokens. `exact` (a mind's activated lore, §24.5) keeps only the exact hits, as World Info activates entries.
  - **Ranking**, compared in order: exact hits on lookup-table terms, aliases, keywords and titles (in `terms`, or said in `query`); links to what the step is about; topics asked for or named in the query; the query's words in the entry (stop words dropped, each word counted at most 3 times); the author's priority. Ties go by id. Of a group's entries only the best is returned.
  - **Snippets** are `Title: text`, cut at a word to the token budget, with the caller's counter: the host tokenizer's count once it has counted that text for the user (fetched in the background, as for sections, §6.3), else the section estimate.
  - **Visibility is enforced inside it:** a secret entry whose gate doesn't hold reaches only the purposes in its audience, never a `PROSE_STEPS` purpose or an `effects` template; a gate that is missing or can't be evaluated never holds; once it holds, the entry reads as public. A `known_by` entry follows §7 for its links and fails closed. Read as a character (a mind, `as`), a secret entry is read only when it is theirs (`loreHeldBy`), gate or no gate (§24.5). The GM (`purpose: "gm"`) reads everything.
- **Policy per step** (`StepConfig.lore`: `{policy, tokens, transport?}`): `none`, `prefetch`, `lookup` or `prefetch+lookup`. A settings file without one reads with its own preset's values (Custom fills in from Standard), and a step no preset configures prefetches 300 tokens. Presets: Lean prefetches 200 tokens and never looks up; Standard gives `interpret`, `extract`, `npc.choose` and `judge` both at 400; Full both at 600 for those and `contradiction`, and a 400-token prefetch to `brief` and `outline`. The minds steps have none: what a mind reads of the lore is set in Settings → Minds (§24.6).
- **Prefetch:** before a step's call, the index is searched with the step's own inputs: the player's message, the reply, the POV, its place and the places holding it, the people it can see there, the visible tracks' labels, stages and cues, a decision's agents and options, or a judged action's label, targets and place with the player's words. The hits go into the prompt as a "World lore" block, or into the state a Decision call reads, at no extra call. A world without lore changes no prompt.
- **Lookups** (JSON helper steps, through quiet generation): the model is offered the lore tools (`lore_search`, `lore_get`, `lore_list`; JSON mode is off while they are) or, on a connection seen refusing tools, the two-phase `{"need": [...]}` form. The host has no tool-role messages, so a round's results (each round within the step's lore tokens, at most 3 asks) go back in a follow-up user message and the call is made again, after the model's ask replayed with only the calls the round answered. A `lore_list` answer names at most 12 entries, each costing the round's tokens as a snippet does, and says how many more there are; a call to a tool that isn't a lore tool is refused, never run as a search. The instructions name up to 12 of the lookup table's topics, and only those the step can read an entry under, so a topic only secrets it can't read are filed under stays unnamed.
  - **Transport:** `auto` uses tools until a connection refuses them. A tool call that fails non-transiently is asked again in the need form on the round already held, and once that works the refusal is kept per connection, in memory and in `userStorage/lore-tools.json`. A step may pin `tools` or `need`.
  - **Budget:** each round is one call on the phase's lookup budget (§5.3), held before the model is offered it and released when it answers instead, so parallel steps never overdraw it; lookups therefore go to steps in priority order. A round starts only within the lookup window and while a third of the phase's time is left. A step that can't hold one gets no tools or need form and answers with its prefetch. Rounds are marked as lookups in the call meter.
- **Decision pre-pass:** the Decisions API takes no tools, so a step on the Decision connection whose policy looks up gets one bounded helper call on the helper model first (§5.3), asking what lore the decision needs (tools or the need form), on a lookup round. It never answers the decision; its hits join the prefetch in the decision's state.
- **Traces:** each step records its searches, the entries it was given with their token cost, its lookups, the transport and why a lookup didn't happen, for the inspector (§6.5), within what the player may know (§7).
- **Secrecy:** §7.

## 6. Context control: the engine's sections

The engine no longer selects user lore for the narrator; its own helpers read world lore through the engine's search (§5.4), and the narrator gets the lore about where the POV is in the Place section (§6.11) and otherwise only what `brief` and the prose `outline` write after reading public entries. In a world chat the engine run writes the turn's sections into the world's own book just before the reply (§5.1, §6.4), and touches no other book. Every other book's entries, and recursion, are left to native World Info, but for the entries the interceptor switches for one prompt (§6.1).

### 6.1 The World Info interceptor (lore selection retired)
The interceptor no longer builds candidates from user books (with pins, D19), skips pass-through books, pre-ranks a hit set capped at 64, asks the `select` step with its per-candidate cache (D13), spends a lore budget, votes user entries off for relevance, suppresses adopted sources (D11), syncs world lore into the engine book, overrides recursion or warns about Council World Info. Native World Info decides user entries in world chats, Council's included, with two exceptions, both for the prompt at hand only (`backend/wi-interceptor.ts`, registered at start, priority 100). In a world chat's every assembly (the context carries no generation type, and keeping a secret off is right in any), before activation: an entry a secret holds back (`secrecyParts`, §4.2: its own `BookLoreFields`, the world entries indexed from it or from the original it is a copy of, and every secret its text is, matched by the text the host sends with each entry, whatever book holds it, one the host builds from a card for the prompt included) is voted `disabled` until every secret's gate holds on the head state as the narrator sees it (`LedgerService.narratorState`: `renderState` of the chain, the turn's planned events included, a prepared turn the guard let a generation take included before that generation adopts it), then `enabled` when LRE stored it off (a secret of its own, or a copy that was on before it was kept), which switches it on; one only its text holds back stays as its author stored it. An entry the turn's sections carried (`Delivered.sources`, §6.11, recorded when the guard fills the book and dropped when it clears it or vacates the book) is voted `disabled`, so its text reaches the narrator once. The engine's own entries are never held back. It reads the user's books only once LRE's first read of them has finished (`readyLoreIndex`): that one can take longer than the host's 10 seconds, so a prompt before it ends (an Impersonate or a dry run right after a restart, before any request or engine run) doesn't wait for it, and every entry of the user's books is voted off for that prompt. It fails closed: when the state can't be read, every entry a secret holds is voted off; while some book of the user's was never read, every entry of the user's books is; when the interceptor throws, every entry its own fields and the secrets as last read hold; and when it runs past the host's 10 seconds the host keeps the entries as stored, where a secret one and its copies are off (an entry the host builds from a card is stored nowhere, so then nothing holds it). World lore stays in the world document (§4.2), where the helpers read it (§5.4).

### 6.2 The section budget
- The slots spend the **section budget** (§5.3) in rank order. **Outcome** (rank 0) is never evicted; a later slot that doesn't fit is clipped, left out for that turn and shown as clipped in the inspector.
- The inspector's budget-used figure is the section budget spent; only an Outcome that alone exceeds the budget can take it past the total.
- **Hint lines** (a renderer marked `hint`: Stance's cues, §6.6; People's usual routines, §6.10) ride last: once every section has its place, each section's hints go in if what is left of the budget covers them, else the section goes without them, and a section with hints alone isn't sent. Clipping, the sizes and the budget-used figure count a section's own lines only, so an off-screen change (a flag set, a lore gate opening) never shows in what the player sees. A hint is never a fact: the helpers' fact lines (`factLines`) leave every hint out.

### 6.3 Slots and rendering
- **Compact lines,** at most one per fact. A section costs the token count the host's tokenizer gives for its exact rendered text (header and bullets included), cached per user and text, since the host counts with each user's own main model. Until the host has counted that text for that user, or when it cannot within a second, an estimate stands in: 3.2 characters per token, plus 2 for the header, 3 for each line break and 1 for each number, which stays at or above the host tokenizers on the shipped worlds' sections. A count that is not a finite number from 0 to 4 times that padded estimate is ignored, so the estimate stands in and a bogus count cannot clip every section. Counts are fetched in the background, so writing the sections never waits on the host.
- **Dynamic values never form a macro.** World Info content is macro-evaluated by the host, so everything the engine inserts (names, prices, model output, proposals) is neutralized: every brace becomes its fullwidth look-alike (｛ ｝), the host's escape sentinels (the C0 control characters `\x00`–`\x02`, which its evaluator turns back into braces on a second pass) are dropped, and the `<user>`, `<char>` and `<bot>` shorthands are broken with fullwidth angle brackets. Section labels and templates are covered too, and a section template may hold only `{{lines}}`. Breaking only `{{` pairs is not enough: it leaves `{{{…}}}` and escaped braces runnable by the host's lexer, and backslash escaping keeps the text literal but delivers visible backslashes. **Authored lore keeps its macros**, as in native World Info, and a world import lists every macro, sentinel and shorthand in its lore and templates for the player to review before playing.
- **Sizing:** native context clipping sees the real size. Slots are fixed overhead and are never clipped by the host; when the fixed prompt exceeds the context budget, the host drops all chat history, including the latest user turn. Nothing checks the engine budget (§5.3) against the connection's context, so keeping it well inside that is the user's call.
- **A section nothing fills:** a world's section is filled by the packs' renderers for its id, or by the turn itself (Outcome, Montage, Outline, Scene's elapsed time, Minds). One neither feeds is never sent, so the world lint warns about it (`section_unfed`): weather, for one, renders into Scene, not a section of its own.
- **Vehicles** (`vehicles`, the Vehicles pack): what the POV may use, each line saying whose it is. At Vehicles 1, the POV's own, "runs fine" or broken down; from 2, also those of a group he is a member of (his family's), with their owner, fuel and range, and status; from 3, also those assigned or lent to him (`uses` grants; a lend with its due time). An owner the POV can't see reads "someone", and a vehicle he can't see isn't listed. One with no class the tables know (a trailer) has no tank, so it gets no fuel or range. The ones away from his site fold into one line, counted by where they are: "Elsewhere: 4 (3 at the ranch, 1 at the marina)". A site (`siteOf` in `engine/presence.ts`) is a place's outermost enclosing place with nothing spread out on the way up (§6.6), so a house with its garage and driveway is one site and a town isn't. Then other people's vehicles at his site that he can see, at most 8 and the rest counted, each with whose it is (owners through `engine/access`, named as he may know them). In the `scene` scope, one he can't see whose owner is with him is added, marked; in `gm`, every one at his site.

### 6.4 The book, turn by turn
For world chats, a reply's guard (§5.1) writes its turn's sections into the world's own book, and nothing else. The book is shared by every chat of the world, and World Info is read from it as it stands, so it belongs to one owner at a time (`ledger-io.ts`, in memory): an engine run ensuring its entries, a reply's guard writing its turn's sections until that reply's prompt is assembled, an Impersonate's prompt (held empty), or a dry run emptying what an earlier turn left. Every value in it belongs to the turn that wrote it; one that doesn't belong to the generation being assembled never reaches its prompt.
- **Filled and switched on:** each section the turn renders, in its section's entry or its preset outlet's twin (§6.8), as a constant entry with the section's text, from the reply's guard until its prompt is assembled.
- **Switched off and emptied:** every other engine-book entry, so an unused slot, or a lore copy an earlier build left in the book, can't activate natively even if someone re-enabled it in the host's UI. Once the reply's prompt is assembled (`GENERATION_IN_PROGRESS`), or the reply ends before that, the turn's entries are emptied and switched off too, right away rather than behind the chat's queue. A turn waiting for its reply holds nothing in the book.
- **Taking the book:** a chat's own reply or Impersonate gives way when a newer generation of that chat arrives (Lumiverse runs one per chat, so theirs is over though its end was never heard). Anything else is waited for, never written over: an engine run ensuring the book, a reply's guard writing it and that reply's prompt, an Impersonate's prompt, entries being emptied. A prepared turn holds nothing, so the chats of a world prepare their replies side by side and none gives way to another.
- **Left on by an earlier run:** a restart mid-reply or a clear the host refused can leave sections in the book with no owner. The next reply's guard switches them off as it writes; a generation with no turn of its own (Impersonate, a dry run) empties them first, reading a book the engine hasn't read since it started; when the host won't empty them, it stops, and the chat says why (§5.1).
- **Placement** is the entry's own (§4.2): the section's Position, moved when it changes, so a chat on an older revision gets its revision's placement while it holds the book.
- **Nothing else:** no entry of another book is touched, and no recursion override is set. Recursion follows the user's World Info settings, so a section's text, like any constant's, can trigger a user entry. Sections carry no unrevealed truth (§6.3, §7), so this changes prompt content, not secrecy.
- **Leftover lore:** a world lore entry an earlier build synced into the book is deleted the next time the book is ensured (commit, import, Upgrade, attach, an engine run).
- **What the book can't hold back:** it holds a turn's sections only while its reply's guard writes them and that reply's prompt is assembled, and a generation the guard can't hold off sees them then: a dry run in another chat of the world (a preview can't be made to wait, and never reaches a model), or a generation that starts while the extension restarts in that moment. The next guarded generation or engine run empties what such a restart left; Spindle gives the extension no shutdown hook to empty them first.

### 6.5 Inspector and limits
- **Inspector:** per generation, the engine sections with their token cost, clipping and delivery, the section budget used, and the activation confirmation. A section left out over the section budget shows a "clipped" badge with "left out: over the section budget" and is never shown as delivered. It keeps the last 10 engine runs per chat, with each section's delivery, in `userStorage/runs/<chat>/inspector.json`, read when the chat is first viewed, so the history and its badges survive a restart; inspector dry runs stay out of the chat history. Its warnings cover the D32 preset lint and journal notices.
- **Lore traces** (§5.4): each record also carries, per model step of its generation, the step's searches with what each matched, the entries it was given with their token cost and whether they were cut, its lookup rounds, the transport and why a lookup didn't happen; the reply's steps join the record after the reply, and the history keeps them. The Context panel shows them to the player, so they carry only what the player may know. A secret entry whose gate doesn't hold isn't in them at all: neither listed nor counted in what a search matched. An `npc.choose` trace shows only whose choice it is, by name ("Dev's choice"), never the options it searched with, which are private plans; for the same reason it is `private`: its model's lookups show as their kind alone, no search says how many entries it matched, and the entries it was given aren't listed, since their titles, number and token cost would all say something of those plans. A batch of choices with no one the POV may know has no trace. A step that may read a secret whose gate doesn't hold shows each search, read and list its model asked for as its kind and what it found, never its text. Any other search or title that carries a secret's text, or names someone the POV doesn't know or a place or thing it can't see (read as a `judge` reason is, `namesAny`: a person by any word of their name, however short in another script), is withheld, and so is a lookup the model asked for that names a secret entry (its id, title, an alias, a key, a lookup-table term or a topic it is filed under). Words the player or the reply said that turn are never withheld: that would only show which of them are secret. The history is stored as shown, and read back by the same rules: one kept before them (`v: 1`) shows its searches' kinds and counts without their text, one that lists a secret entry isn't shown, and an `npc.choose` trace reads as one kept now, uncounted and with no entries.
- **Other extensions' World Info interceptors** may switch the engine's entries off for a generation; those sections then show as not delivered. What they do to user entries is theirs. The README lists this caveat.
- **If the backend stalls:** the guard isn't answered, Lumiverse stops the generation (the guard is required), and nothing goes out with stale or missing sections. A turn waiting for its reply holds nothing in the book, so a generation that goes ahead without the guard (the extension restarting, a Lumiverse that doesn't require it) finds no engine section but what a reply's guard was writing at that moment (§6.4).

### 6.6 Stance
The **Stance** slot (`stance`, written by the Cast life pack) tells the narrator how the people with the POV stand toward it and what they are about, in one or two lines each. It sits beside People (chat depth 1) and takes default rank 9, after every fact section a world relied on before it, so on a tight section budget it is left out before them, never displacing one; only the sections added since (Place at 11 and Property at 12, §6.11) rank after it, so they give way first. A world that wants it sooner gives it a lower rank in its own sections. Its cue lines are hint lines (§6.2): they go in only from what the budget has left after every section, and the Context panel's size for Stance counts its people lines alone, since the cues change with what the POV doesn't know.
- **Who:** every person in the POV's scene (`together`, §24.1: the same place, or one at most two levels inside the other with no spread-out place between) that the POV can see (`canSeeEntity`) and who acts. "Acts" is one predicate (`acts` in the pack), today someone not removed, so a person's life state can decide it in one place. Nobody else gets a line, and no line names anyone the POV can't see.
- **People beside it** (the `people` slot, Cast life's lines) names the POV first, so the narrator knows who "you" is, with the needs pressing on it from Cast life 4 ("Alex Rivera (you): hungry, tired, lonely"), then each person in the POV's scene it can see, each followed by their regard toward it in the world's band words (`relationships.voice`; none for a body the POV knows of). The helpers' fact lines (`factLines`) already open with the POV, so they leave out a POV line that carries nothing but its name.
- **What they know of the POV:** whether they know it (a `knows` relation to it, or regard toward it: nobody regards a stranger) and, from Relationships 1 at them, their regard in the world's band words, never numbers: the overall band and, from Relationships 3, each axis whose band differs from it (`relationships.words`: "knows you; regard: warm (trust: cold)").
- **Intent:** their activity as People reads it (field visibility, an agenda's step as "busy"); for each public agenda they own, its current stage's cue, or the stage label Cast shows when it has none; and for an LOD1 agent from Cast life 3, the block of today's plan that their own recorded choice settled (`npc.choose`, or `castlife.decide`), the one under way or the next, named as Cast names the day (a block of an agenda that isn't public is "Busy").
- **Cues:** behaviour the author wrote for a secret, never the secret.
