  - A normal turn stages its reply empty with `MESSAGE_SENT` before `GENERATION_STARTED` and saves it with `MESSAGE_EDITED`, or creates it with `MESSAGE_SENT` at the save (P4). Regenerate, swipe and both Edit-and-Send modes stage with `MESSAGE_SWIPED` `added` and save with `updated`. A Continue saves with `MESSAGE_EDITED`, twice when its prefix re-renders.
  - `GENERATION_STOPPED` is `{generationId, chatId, content}`, with the suffix only for a Continue. Its save comes first.
  - Swipe navigation stays live on any message while a reply streams. Branch creation is silent. Event payloads carry no `userId`; it arrives as the handler's second argument. A reply can be saved empty. A restart removes a staged reply. A swipe delete leaves `spindle_metadata` unshifted. A message keeps its `index_in_chat` for life: a new one takes one past the highest, so a deletion leaves a gap and nothing is renumbered. A page saves its settings as one `SETTINGS_UPDATED` batch naming its keys, read through the backend's own decoder.
  - The host's macro re-render of the turn's user messages (P4), which the reference host did not show, is generated in both variants (present and absent).
- **Randomized operation sequences** (seeded generator; failures shrink to a minimal sequence). Operations:
  - send, regenerate and swipe (Re-run and Re-narrate);
  - Continue on the head, on a non-head message and with a stop;
  - Edit-and-Send in both modes;
  - earlier swipe navigate, add, delete and `PUT`;
  - middle and latest delete;
  - branch and import;
  - chip confirm, ✎ fix and materialization;
  - realism, time and Upgrade markers;
  - Keep, Discard and convert;
  - restart, and foreign or frontend clobbering;
  - any of these interleaved inside an in-flight turn, while it streams and during its post pass;
  - after the rest, from a stream of their own (so every sequence before them plays as it did): turns the engine plans with life changes (the `life` effect and a rule that tells of a death) and queued actions that stop at a check, a failure or an interruption, GM life edits, and the Re-runs, edits, swipes, Keep choices, Upgrades and in-flight turns that move them onto another view. The suite asserts that life changes, tellings and queues of each kind happen.
- **Oracle:** after every operation, the optimized fold equals the reference fold (state, Journal notes and Keep report), and the invariants the runner checks hold: L1–L3, L5–L9, L11, L13–L20, L31, L34 and L38–L47 (`test/support/ops.ts`). The rest are checked case by case: L4, L10 and L12 in the ledger scenarios and reference cases, L27 and L37 in the fake-host tests, L48 in unit and fake-host tests, and L21–L24, L26, L28, L29, L32, L33 and L36 in unit tests; L30 has no test of its own.
- **Volume:** a default run executes a fixed-seed sample of 200 sequences of up to 60 operations; `LRE_PROPERTY_RUNS`, `LRE_PROPERTY_OPS`, `LRE_PROPERTY_SEED`, `LRE_PROPERTY_BASE` and `LRE_PROPERTY_FROM` set the count, the longest sequence, one seed to replay, the seed base and the first seed index, so a long run can be split into shards, and `LRE_PROPERTY_COVERAGE` names a file for a shard's coverage counts, so the shards together are held to the whole run's coverage (`test/support/coverage.ts`). The release gate runs the fixed sample in ten shards of 20, held together to its coverage, and 200 fresh sequences in five shards of 40, each held to the minds' coverage as any 40 sequences are. `test/ledger/regressions.test.ts` replays the fixed regression seed set (every seed that has ever failed, shrunk) on every run of the suite. The ledger gate passes only when all of them pass.
- **Named invariants.**

| Invariant | Property |
|---|---|
| **L1 Reference equality** | The optimized fold (cache, overlay, re-convergence) equals the reference fold. |
| **L2 Switch away and back** | Switch away from any earlier swipe, do anything there (materialization, ✎ fix, chip, Continue, markers, turns, conversions), switch back ⇒ the original fold, exactly. Everything made there is inert and listed "made on another branch". |
| **L3 No pause on a switch** | An upstream active-swipe change or message removal never pauses; only a user-message content edit does. |
| **L4 Classification by state** | Classification is a function of the chain-snapshot diff, never of event names: a user-message edit, an assistant edit or swipe `PUT` (on the active swipe, or on an inactive one once it becomes active), an explicit-`message_id` regenerate and a middle delete classify as content edit, assistant edit, turn and removal. |
| **L5 Off-path commit** | A turn whose chain changed while it was in flight never commits on-path: an upstream swipe change gives a rebased view, and a user-message edit, including one to its own user messages, gives the pause. An assistant edit leaves it on-path. Back on the chain it was planned on (its swipes as they stand after deletions), it folds on its path with nothing failing. |
| **L6 Host writes are not edits** | The host's macro re-render of the turn's own user messages and a Continue's own save never classify as content edits. |
| **L7 Re-fold equals validation** | Every committed event and `post` entry, re-folded, sees exactly the state it was validated against (its fold prefix, or the head's fold up to its `seq`). |
| **L8 One window per turn** | No write made inside a turn's window is journaled before the window closes; each then takes its `seq` and folds after the turn's events, and detail shown as "pending" is recorded once. |
| **L9 Persistent `seq`** | `seq` comes from the journal's counter, strictly increases across restarts and clobbering, and never names two different entries (a Re-narrate copy keeps its source's `seq`). |
| **L10 Fold order equals planning order** | A swipe folds as it was planned: live markers ≤ `markersApplied`, `events`, then `post` entries and later markers by `seq`. A marker made after a swipe never changes how that swipe's events fold. |
| **L11 Re-narrate** | A Re-narrate folds exactly as its source up to the reply; each copied entry passes re-validation against its own prefix or is a Journal drop; its `narr` entry folds after everything in effect when it was narrated. When the source cannot be reproduced, it is a Re-run. |
| **L12 Re-run** | A Re-run records a fresh seed and re-folds identically; across generated trials a 50% check passes within tolerance. |
| **L13 Continue reads only its suffix** | A Continue never re-reads or re-applies effects already in the record, and its `cont` entry folds after every entry journaled before it started. |
| **L14 Accepted Continue** | A non-head Continue, one on a message without a record, a stopped one and one whose prefix was re-rendered append no events to any record and move no later path; the target holds the text as `accepted`. |
| **L15 Stopped-Continue target** | A stopped Continue's text lands on the message found by content, whatever else was edited or written meanwhile; no record is orphaned and nothing pauses. |
| **L16 No silent drop** | Every stored entry the fold does not apply has a Journal note or a Keep-report line (dropped, inert, Re-narrate drop, tombstoned). |
| **L17 No resurrection** | After a Discard, no re-assert, restart, clobber recovery, swipe-index shift or later operation restores a discarded record. |
| **L18 Durability** | After foreign or frontend clobbering and a restart, records, markers and held writes are re-asserted and the fold is unchanged. |
| **L19 Deletion keeps live markers** | Deleting a middle or the latest message keeps its live markers' effect on later folds; its inert markers go with it, journaled. |
| **L20 Starting markers** | Each chat has exactly one set, on the first planned record that holds events, live on every path, each journaled as the marker check gives it (a world's half-minute tick as the minute it plays); a chat an earlier build started keeps them as that build journaled them, since its paths were hashed with those. Later Settings changes, switches, Discards and deletions never change it, and a copy gets them where its chat does. |
| **L21 Concrete deltas** | Events hold deltas, never final values, and a view re-applies them. |
| **L22 The ledger balances** | The trial balance is zero after every operation, at every level mix, including external-economy postings and payroll withholding. |
| **L23 Uniform representation** | Demote, change and promote leave aggregates and the trial balance intact and restore nothing stale. |
| **L24 Materialization** | A first promotion from a band lands inside the band. With unchanged pins, detail is identical across extension updates; new tables or generators arrive only through a `migrate` marker. |
| **L26 Elapsed time** | Every unpaused turn advances the clock by its elapsed time; a time-control change applies by `seq`, never to a swipe planned before it. |
| **L27 No double time** | A swipe's clock advance equals its elapsed time plus advances for narrated time beyond what it had already applied; timelapse and Speed-through turns propose none. |
| **L28 Advance equals timelapse** | An applied `time.advance` to T folds like a timelapse to T the story holds the POV through (`povRoutine` false, as a typed span is), with every interrupt class at `note`; an advance whose catch-up cannot run whole applies nothing. |
| **L29 Segmentation** | Skipping N days = N one-day steps = any segmentation of the same timelapse; every segment advances at least one slot. |
| **L30 Interpret re-simulates** | After `interpret` changes the action, its duration or its activity, the recorded elapsed time and simulation equal those of a turn planned with that action directly. |
| **L31 Determinism** | The same operations and seeds give the same fold, whatever the wall clock, lane scheduling or chunking. |
| **L32 Bounds** | Every applied model-proposed change is within its field bound and channel (§9.4); prices come from the catalog or the category band. |
| **L33 Overflow** | A record past its size target coarsens or chunks, and round-trips without truncation. |
| **L34 Previews record nothing** | Dry Run, the GM view, the inspector, audits and playtests leave the ledger and journal unchanged. |
| **L36 Kits** | Kit completeness is derived from template plus overrides; taking a piece adds one override. |
| **L37 Context on the fake host** | The engine run fills, places and switches nothing outside the world's own book and sets no recursion override; inside it, only the turn's sections are switched on, as constant entries, and every other engine entry is off and empty. |
| **L38 Assistant edits keep the world** | Editing an assistant message, on the head or earlier, by edit or swipe `PUT`, active or inactive, never changes the fold unless Re-read edit is tapped; Re-read on an earlier message makes later records rebased views. |
| **L39 User edits pause** | Editing an earlier user message pauses later records until Keep or Discard. |
| **L40 No negative accounts** | No own account folds below zero on any path (a Keep conversion is the exception); a rebased view drops what it cannot cover, and its dropped notes and Keep lines agree. |
| **L41 Groups land whole** | Within a record, a group of events is dropped whole or kept whole, never in part. |
| **L42 Snapshots** | A cold fold that starts from the chat's stored snapshots equals the full fold. |
| **L43 A Re-run carries its fixes** | Every live ✎ fix or GM edit of the swipe a Re-run was made from is on the new record with its `seq`, events and undos, anchored at the record's path, or is a Journal drop; nothing else is copied. |
| **L44 An Upgrade keeps what play changed** | An Upgrade with picks never takes a revision's value over one play changed, unless the owner picked it. |
| **L45 Copies fold alike** | A branch or an import folds every message it copied as its chat does: the same folds, paths and state, from the message metadata alone, including a copy of a chat an earlier build wrote. |
| **L46 Detail materializes once** | On any path, an entity's detail in a domain is materialized at most once (a Keep conversion aside), however its entries are carried, kept, held or rebased. |
| **L47 A reply only where it can start** | Where the guard stops generations, a reply for an earlier message (Edit and Send in place, a swipe of it) is refused: nothing is generated and no message's swipes change (§5.1). |
| **L48 Set back, never rerun** | After a ✎ fix sets the clock back, nothing that ran up to where it stood is undone or runs again: passing that window again, by any way time moves, adds only the clock's move, and past it the world steps as it would have. Undoing the fix restores the clock and the mark. |

**Loop-sim** (including mixed-level and Story-default scenarios)
- the clock advances every turn unless paused; timelapses stop only on `stop`-class interrupts;
- shown odds equal real odds;
- the books balance;
- calls and tokens stay within budget;
- no secret truth reaches slots;
- no double-booking;
- no stale restore.

**Live checks** (narrator and helper JSON on the chat connection; Decision steps on the Decision connection; the measured model is a floor, D31)
- the host and model viability checks (§18);
- the performance measurements (§18.5).

**Test runs** (README "Tests"; `scripts/test/run.ts`)
- **During a change:** `bun run test` runs the test files that cover what changed since the branch left `main`, committed or not (`scripts/test/select.ts`). A changed test, the tests named after a changed module and the tests that read a changed doc, template or table always run; past them come the tests that import what changed, then the rest of its area, then the other tests that reach it through their imports, nearest first and cheapest first, while their recorded time (`test/timings.json`) fits a minute. The import graph is dense (almost every test reaches the engine through the packs, and every fake-host test reaches the backend through the rig), so a hub such as the runtime or the rig runs its nearest tests, not every test that reaches it. A change to bunfig, tsconfig or the lockfile runs every file. The property suite runs only when its own code changed, at 20 sequences.
- **Before a commit:** `bun run check`: both typechecks, `docs/REFERENCE.md` and the starter worlds current, the templates valid, then the run above.
- **At release:** `bun run test:release`, the gate: those checks, every test file, and the property suite on the fixed sample and on fresh seeds (above), in parallel across the cores. Test files run one process per core with a 30-second timeout per test, since a busy machine stretches a two-second test past Bun's default of five.
- Plain `bun test` still runs every file in one process, the property suite's fixed sample whole, in about five minutes.

**Acceptance**
- Visible checks on the reference host (D28), at phone width, with screenshots, and a live check of the complete extension before a release.
- Decision-API and live checks use the Decision connection (D27).

## 15. Reuse from Warp and the studio seam (D1, D23)

[Warp](https://github.com/japolino/warp) is japolino's earlier roleplay engine. I reused parts of it with his permission for this non-commercial project, and the `NOTICE` file lists every file.
- **Adapted code** (carried over and changed): `engine/expr.ts` (the safe formula language, with hard limits, a typed limit error, a shareable step budget and a finite-number result), `engine/dice.ts` (the dice notation and roller, with an injected generator, exact outcome distributions and bounded input) and `engine/decide.ts` (the decision question and answer shapes, with strict validation and a canonical option order).
- **Design only, with no Warp code retained:** the per-swipe turn record in message metadata and the path hash (the ledger, §4.3), a small deterministic fingerprint (`hash.ts`), revision-counted state pushes (`push.ts`), a fake host that drives the real backend in tests, a whole-loop simulator as a quality gate, and pan and zoom kept as pure functions (`map-math.ts`).
- **Written for the adaptation:** `engine/rng.ts`, a seeded generator (sfc32 seeded from the hash of a seed string) that replaces the generator inside Warp's dice module. MurmurHash3 (Austin Appleby) and sfc32 (Chris Doty-Humphrey) are public-domain algorithms.
- **Looked at and not used:** Warp's overlay layout, world, quests, companion-life and companions test files. A comparison found no shared code or structure.
- Warp's reasons for cutting scope are inferred from its commits; its design documents are not published.

**Studio seam (fixed now, so the studio needs no breaking change later)**
- **World format:** `lre-world-v1`, with its JSON Schema (`schemas/lre-world-v1.schema.json`) and the map's (`src/shared/lre-map-v1.schema.json`), both in the package `exports`, and per-pack versions.
- **`src/studio-api.ts`:** imports only pure pack `core` modules (lint-enforced) and is exported through the package `exports` map as `lumi-rp-engine/studio`. It provides load, validate, migrate, lint, a new world, the revision id, the export file, Playtest, Check world, the Sheet Designer's preview, the `lre-state-v1` payload, the schema reader, the Studio bridge's names and payloads, and the format, engine and pack versions. `docs/STUDIO.md` is its manual.
- **`lre-state-v1` window event payload:** `{engineVersion, worldFormat, packs:{id:version}, inbound}`, announced when the frontend loads, when the pack set changes and when a `lre-state-request-v1` event asks. `inbound` lists `file` and `rpc:lumi_rp_engine_studio.offer`.
- **Install from a studio:** an export file through Build's **Import an lre-world-v1 file…**, which makes a new world and lists its macros for review; or LumiRPEngine Studio's offer, through the Studio bridge.
- **The Studio bridge** (`shared/studio-bridge.ts`, `backend/studio.ts`; STUDIO.md "The Studio bridge"). A shared RPC read carries no request and no user, so it carries no commands: each side publishes endpoints per user and the other pulls.
  - **Engine → Studio, read-only.** Per user: the world list, each world's head and each revision, and the world chats' summaries. Channels name ids by their bytes in hex (a segment takes only `[a-z0-9_-]`; user and world ids are mixed case). Every endpoint answers `lumi_rp_engine_studio` only, and the summaries' read policy needs `chats`. A summary is built from the view last pushed to the chat, so it carries only what the player sees (place, clock, cash, the people present with their life state as the POV knows it, the open quests): the Studio is an authoring tool and reads the world document for the rest, but the summary never becomes a way around the player layer. A secret track goes by the quest log's own stand-in id (§7), since its own id is the author's and may name the secret.
  - **Studio → engine, by offer.** The Studio publishes the user's pending offer (a document, `new` or `revision` of a world, the revision it was taken from, an optional chat to attach) and its frontend dispatches `lre-studio-offer-v1`; Build reads it, reviews it (counts, lint, macros, what blocks it) and installs it only on the player's **Install**, as the offer reviewed: a new world as an import makes one, or a new revision under the world's own id, whose chats move to it only through an Upgrade. A revision is refused while Build holds an unsaved draft of the world, which a later Commit would put back over it. When the world's head has moved since the revision was taken (its `baseRevision`, or none given), Build warns rather than blocks: revisions are immutable, so the head it passes over stays, with its chats, but installing takes the player's explicit **Install anyway** (`acceptMovedHead` on the request), which also catches a head that moves after the review. Install names back the hash of the offer reviewed and is refused when the offer has changed since. Installs run one at a time per user, and the offer is marked installed as soon as its world is written, so it installs once however often Install is pressed. The chat the offer names gets Play's Attach and its confirmation when it's open and plays no world.

## 16. Host gaps and upstream follow-ups (D21)

These are the places where Lumiverse itself would make LumiRPEngine simpler or safer. I plan to propose them upstream after the first release; the extension ships with the caveats below documented.

| # | Gap and proposal | Size |
|---|---|---|
| G1 | Generation and target ids in context-handler and World Info interceptor contexts (plus `generationType`/`dryRun` for the latter); `generationId` on `WORLD_INFO_ACTIVATED` | ~20–40 LOC |
| G2 | **Namespaced, merge-safe, swipe-aligned extension message metadata** (required for coexistence) | ~10–120 LOC |
| G3 | Keep the prompt-assembly worker for unaffected chats and users (`match`-gated interceptors or an `unregister` API) | ~15–80 LOC |
| G4 | World Info interceptor abort signal and flags; opt-in fail-closed mode | ~25–40 LOC |
| G5 | Council World Info through the interceptor chain | ~40–60 LOC |
| G6 | Doc fixes; the `match.isDryRun` → `dryRun` bug; `WORLD_INFO_ACTIVATED.stats.evictedByBudget` misses budget evictions; the published types lag the host | Small |
| G7 | Entry ids on World Info-derived prompt messages | ~15–25 LOC |
| G8 | Forced entries skip inclusion-group competition | ~10–20 LOC |
| G9 | Keep `spindle_metadata` out of the light message list | Small |
| G10 | A message read without `extra` (reasoning), or by range: `getMessages` sends every message with all its reasoning, 174 MB on an 844-message chat, so the backend keeps its own copy (§9.6) | Small |
| G11 | A Regenerate of a chosen reply from an extension's page: a validated `{messageId}` on the `action-regenerate` or `action-swipe-right` command, running `useSwipeAction`'s path (`generate` with `message_id` and `generation_type: "swipe"`, and the store's streaming hooks). Commands take no arguments today (`applyHostAction` rejects them) and the store isn't open to extensions, so the restart after an engine run can start only the last reply, and a reply for an earlier message (Edit and Send in place, a swipe of it) is refused (§5.1) | ~40–80 LOC |
| G12 | An extension's drawer tab keeps its place through the extension's own reload. On an update the loader unloads the old frontend before the new one registers its tab again, about 50 ms later and under a new id; in between, the drawer falls back to its first tab and stores that as the selection (`ViewportDrawer.tsx`). So a page that had LumiRPEngine's tab open shows Profile after an update, with the update notice one tap away, and at phone width the re-added button lands just past the edge of the tab strip. Either the drawer stores no fallback for a tab that is only reloading, or the loader swaps the old tab for the new one instead of unloading first | Small |
| G13 | An extension reconnecting a Lumiverse MCP server profile. Spindle's `mcp.servers` (`src/spindle/worker-host-mcp-api.ts`) lists, gets, creates and connects, but has no reconnect or disconnect, and `connect` on a profile already connected returns its status and does nothing (`handleConnect`, line 123 at core `7524546f`). The profiles it returns (`toSpindleMcpServerDTO`) carry no command or arguments, so an extension can't tell which profile runs its own file. Lumiverse's MCP client lists a server's tools only when it connects and ignores `notifications/tools/list_changed`, so Reconnect in Lumiverse (§23) asks the server's own process to exit and relies on that client connecting a closed stdio server again: `client.onclose` schedules a reconnect (`src/services/mcp-client-manager.ts`, lines 138–143), about 1 s after a first close (line 66), with the profile's unchanged command. If a future Lumiverse stops reconnecting a closed server, the server doesn't come back within the user's wait and its line falls back to the manual instruction (Disconnect and Connect it in Settings → MCP Servers). The fix: a `servers.reconnect(id)` behind `mcp_servers`, and the client re-listing on `list_changed` | Small |

**Shipped caveats** (Settings and README):
- While the extension is enabled, prompt assembly for **all users on the host** runs on the main process. On a 1,000-message chat the measured overhead was about 23 ms per prompt build (§18.5).
- Coexistence with other message-metadata writers (G2).
- In world chats, other extensions' World Info interceptors may switch the engine's entries off for a generation; the inspector then shows those sections not delivered. The engine doesn't touch user entries.
- In world chats, a reply for an earlier message (Edit and Send in place, a swipe of an older reply through the API) is refused until G11; Edit and Send as a branch, and Regenerate of the last reply, work.

## 17. Delivery (D16)
The extension is delivered and checked as a whole: nothing is deferred to a later release. The ledger gate (§14) covers the ledger, the test suite and type checks cover the rest, and the visible checks on the reference host (D28) cover every surface at phone width.

## 18. Host checks, thresholds and fallbacks
The design rests on assumptions about the host and the models, checked on the reference host (D28) with synthetic volumes and live model calls. Each check below has its threshold and its fallback.

| Check | Verdict |
|---|---|
| 1 Correlation | Pass. `select` p95 inside the interceptor: 1.13 s (helper), 0.32 s (decisions). `select` was later retired, and then the interceptor itself (D9). |
| 2 Delivery | Partial: evictions unreported, escaping visible, placement chat-depth only, history wiped when fixed overhead exceeds the budget, card outlets unverified (§5.1, §6.3, §6.4, §4.2). |
| 3 Ledger, host side | Partial: every host-observable item passes. Engine-fold items need the engine (D34); the user-message re-render was not observed (P4, D35). |
| 4 Model viability | Partial; **extraction precision failed** (§9.4; D4 per-type defaults). Decision-path findings: D10, D30. Empty replies: D32. The numbers are a floor (D31). |
| 5 Performance | Pass, except the rebased-view timing, which needs the engine fold (D34). Timelapse record size binds (§4.3). |
| 6 Published types | Pass, with differences (§3). |
| 7 UI | Pass, with fixes (§12; D33). |

1. **Correlation.**
   - The context handler and the interceptor see the same chain for normal, regenerate, swipe, continue and Edit-and-Send generations. The engine doesn't rely on it: the engine runs before the reply, and the guard only checks the turn it prepared (§5.1).
   - A dry run during a live generation.
   - **Stop, then Regenerate:** the host lets the stopped generation's interceptor finish, and that interceptor writes nothing to the ledger or the journal (§5.1); the in-flight `select` is aborted (`select` since retired).
   - The `spindle.assemble` path.
   - `select` latency inside the interceptor, p95 ≤ 6 s (`select` since retired).
2. **Delivery.**
   - Disable all; enable and force stored-disabled engine entries.
   - **Disabled user constants and disabled keyword entries stay off.**
   - **Another extension's forced entry survives.**
   - Escaped slots; `selectionContent`; placement; deliverability resolution and "unverified".
   - Groups, including grouped pins.
   - Forced entries are never evicted; pass-through evictions are reported.
   - Clipping with large slots; recursion off; fail-closed on a forced timeout.
3. **Ledger.** The host's event order was recorded for every operation the §14 property suite generates; the fake host replays exactly that order.
   - No message writes in flight; Edit-and-Send in both modes.
   - Host macro re-render causes no conflict: the trailing user messages' re-render arrives as `MESSAGE_EDITED` between the reply's `MESSAGE_SENT` and `GENERATION_ENDED` and is accepted (P4); an edit of the turn's own user message while it streams pauses.
   - Foreign-writer and frontend-echo clobbering of records **and markers** is re-asserted.
   - Deleting the latest message moves its markers.
   - Swipe add, delete and navigate; branch; import after a deletion; deleting a message in the middle.
   - Keep report; rebased views (switch, open Money so detail materializes, confirm a chip, change realism, send a turn, switch back: the original state is intact and those entries are listed "made on another branch"; switch again and they are live; a one-tap Discard survives re-assert, restart and a lower swipe's deletion) versus a pausing user-message edit; restart recovery; stop and error partial saves.
   - An earlier swipe switch while a reply streams (the host keeps navigation enabled): the reply commits off-path as a rebased view. Detail opened, a chip confirmed or a marker made while it streams or during its post pass shows "pending" and lands after the reply's events (P2).
   - `/regenerate` with an explicit `message_id` on an earlier message (`MESSAGE_SWIPED` `added`) is a turn there and rebases later records, and a marker made during it attaches to the head; a swipe `PUT` (`MESSAGE_SWIPED` `updated`) on an assistant message is an assistant edit, also on an inactive swipe once it is navigated to.
   - Fold order with marker `seq`: a swipe created after an Upgrade folds identically to how it was planned. `post` entries interleave with later markers. **Upgrade or chip confirm, then Continue:** the `cont` entry folds after them, exactly as validated. **Upgrade, then Re-narrate:** the new swipe folds as the source did up to the reply and its `narr` entry folds after the Upgrade; detail materialized for something only the source's extraction created is dropped with a Journal note.
   - Deleting a **middle** message keeps its markers' effect on later turns.
   - Continue extracts only the suffix and never double-applies. A non-head Continue (explicit `message_id`) and a Continue on the greeting before the first turn are accepted unread, and the save's `MESSAGE_EDITED` before `GENERATION_ENDED` raises no conflict. A Continue on a message whose prefix holds literal braces is detected and accepted unread.
   - Stop during a head and a non-head Continue, with another message edited and another writer's metadata write meanwhile: `GENERATION_STOPPED` carries only the partial suffix and no `messageId`; the text is recorded as `accepted` on the message found by content and raises no conflict.
4. **Model viability** (≥30 labeled samples each):
   - **Chat connection** (narrator, helper JSON and the D10 helper path):
     - helper JSON parse ≥ 0.97 after one repair;
     - extraction precision ≥ 0.90 (measured: 0.74 with strict rules at first; 0.68 overall through the engine's own step, with the per-type results and the resulting `auto` set in §9.4);
     - narrator contradiction ≤ 10%;
     - helper p95 ≤ 6 s;
     - `interpret` top-1 ≥ 0.85 on the helper path, for chats without a Decision connection.
   - **Decision connection** (D30):
     - `interpret` top-1 ≥ 0.85;
     - `decisions.evaluate` p95 ≤ 6 s (a live sample took about 230–300 ms);
     - answer shape: choice or score with probabilities and confidence, as the seeded sampling needs (§5.2);
     - agreement with the helper path for D10, reported.
   - Classifier firing rate: retired with the classifier; `interpret` reads every typed message (§5.2).
   - **Fallback:** recommend confirm-by-default for failing field types. Resolved as D4's per-type defaults.
5. **Performance** (synthetic volumes first, then the real packs):
   - fold ≤ 50 ms at 1k turns and ≤ 250 ms at 5k with a level-4 ledger;
   - a rebased view after an earlier switch ≤ 250 ms over 1k later turns with a cold cache (re-validation plus re-convergence);
   - 30-day catch-up for 20 NPCs ≤ 2 s in process;
   - a default-cap timelapse segment (600 agent-days, interrupts and Montage on) ≤ 2 s in process; the default cap is set from this measurement;
   - interceptor apply ≤ 20 ms (excluding a model call; retired with the interceptor, D9);
   - record size per preset; overflow chunking;
   - chat-open payload growth;
   - main-process assembly cost.
   - **Results with the real packs** (at level 4):

     | Gate | Result |
     |---|---|
     | Fold, 1k turns (≤ 50 ms) | Cold, from a fold snapshot: 19.4–22 ms. Full, no snapshot: 93 ms. Warm: 11–13 ms. Pass. |
     | Fold, 5k turns (≤ 250 ms) | Cold, from a snapshot: 95.8 ms. Full: 576 ms. Warm: 100.8 ms (measured before the prefix memo). Pass. |
     | Rebased view, 1k later turns, cold cache (≤ 250 ms) | 20 ms median, 42.5 ms max. Pass. |
     | 30-day catch-up and timelapse, 20 NPCs (≤ 2 s) | Simulation: 160 ms. Level 4: 115 ms. Pass. |
     | Timelapse record (≤ 32 KB) | Simulation: 29.7 KB. Level 4: 30.1 KB. Pass. |
     | Interceptor apply (≤ 20 ms) | Offline bench: at turn 1000, 11.8 ms median and 12.3 ms p95; at turn 500, 5.6 / 6.0; with the host's token counts fetched in the background (§6.3). Pass. |
     | Record size per preset (mean / p95 / max bytes) | Story: 408 / 704 / 897. Grounded: 410 / 704 / 897. Simulation: 429 / 982 / 1874. Level 4: 429 / 982 / 1874. |
     | Chat-open payload | Turn 0: 22.0 KB. Turns 100 to 1000: 30.7–31.7 KB, flat. |
     | Main-process assembly, 1,000-message chat | Live host: extension on 122 ms median (p95 131), off 99 ms (p95 103), re-enabled 124 ms. Overhead: +23 ms median (+22 to +23 ms across runs). |

     Building the 1,000-turn chat through the runtime takes 14 s with the fold memo; the 5,000-turn chat takes 956 s.

     A release passes the full test suite, `tsc`, a long property run and a scan of `dist`.
6. **Published types** (the host pins 0.6.49; 0.6.50 adds only `ctx.client`): `registerDomDecorator`, `ctx.ui.geometry`, float-widget `setFullscreen`/`persistGeometry`, `entries.setExtension`, `inline_available`, `council.getSettings`, `backendProcesses` (spawn, messaging, heartbeat), `rpcPool`, the range slider.
7. **UI.** Preact plus host components; the drawer at 326 px; the map widget; chips; the Money and Cast panels at 390 px.

## 19. Risks

| Risk | Mitigation |
|---|---|
| Scope (Warp's larger version was cut back) | Six concepts; uniform representation; packs with level contracts; schema-driven editors; boundary lint; gates first; per-block checks. |
| Prompt bloat | Compact renderers; the section budget, with Outcome never evicted; the inspector. User lore follows native World Info and its budget (§6). |
| Latency | A deterministic plan; the guard only checks, in memory; tie-zone-only NPC calls; beats by default; budgets with fallbacks; the call meter. |
| Ledger divergence | Concrete events; fold order; journaled markers and `seq`s; path tags (P1); one in-flight window per turn (P2); rebased views that never rewrite records; Keep re-validation; tombstones; chain snapshot; one queue per chat; recovery; the executable ledger property suite with a reference fold (§14). |
| Coexistence (metadata, other interceptors) | Documented caveats; G2 follow-up. |
| Host-wide worker cost | Disclosure; measured; G3 follow-up. |
| Worker or child stall | Fail-closed engine slots; deterministic-unit bounds; backlog; separate lanes; heartbeat chunking. |
| Decision-provider failures (6 of 53 `decisions.evaluate` calls failed in one test window; the host never retries) | D10: the helper, then engine-only; per-step fallbacks (§5.3). |
| Secret leakage | Visibility; public-only `knows` extraction; prompts that list only what the POV may know; predicate-only secret stages; label lint; batching rule; previews never recorded; player-worded drop lines (§7). |
| Imported or model-supplied text forming a prompt macro | Every inserted value is neutralized (§6.3); section templates hold only `{{lines}}`; authored lore keeps its macros and an import lists them for review. |
| Hostile or malformed data ids and files | Validated ids and keys, own-key lookups, settings conformed to a schema, damaged stored files moved aside and replaced by fresh ones or the defaults (§3, §4). |
| A double tap or a concurrent request | Chip actions and reads run under a per-(user, chat) lock and re-check inside it (§11). |
| Authoring burden | Import from lore; data tables; lazy materialization; templates; schema-driven editors. |
| Payload growth | Compact records; period closes; overflow chunks; view-only materialization; fold snapshots; G9. |
| Timelapse cost | Deterministic unit cap; slot-bounded segments that always advance; heartbeat chunks; utility-only NPC choices; no partial apply. |
| Worlds not portable (D2) | `lre-world-v1` plus lore-book export. |

## 20. Where the design yields to the host

**Principles an extension can't keep whole.** The engine can't be the authority over the chat, because an extension isn't the host, so three principles take a weaker form:
1. **Authority.** LumiRPEngine is a namespaced proposal layer with a versioned export, not the owner of the chat.
2. **Canonical plane.** The ledger rides on message metadata; the shadow journal compensates for the host's weaknesses there (§4.3).
3. **Branching.** Re-narrate versus Re-run is re-render versus re-execute. A chat branch is "branch world state".

**Choices and their reasons.** The sections above describe the engine as it is; each row here names the more obvious design, what the engine does instead, and why.

| The obvious design | What the engine does | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Turn-critical simulation in a child *turn lane* (D9) | In the backend process, inside the engine run, bounded by the same unit caps; the child lane stays for Playtest, Check world, imports and Build with AI (§9.6) | A segment took 38 ms on a child process and 2.5 ms in process |
| Re-narrate always copies its source (P3) | It falls back to a Re-run when the source cannot be reproduced (§4.3) | P3 cannot hold without the source's prefix |
| A `seq` is never reused (L9) | A Re-narrate copy keeps its source's `seq`; L9 reads "never names two different entries" | P3 and L9 conflicted as written |
| An undo cascades | An undo that later on-path events depend on is refused (§11) | Keeps L7 |
| An inspector history kept for diagnostics | The last 10 runs per chat with the delivery badges, stored with the chat (§6.5) | Bounded, and a restart keeps it |
| World document without a currency | Adds `currency`; Build drafts with Commit and Discard (§4.1) | Authoring needed them |
| Import: accept, edit or reject each proposal | Accept or reject; edit afterwards in the editors (§9.7) | Bounded like extraction |
| Data tables: worlds can replace them with another region's | The table editor edits a table; replacement is not built (§9.7) | Not on the critical path |
| World lore without a world-document field | `WorldDoc.lore` and an editor collection, kept in the world and read by the engine's helpers, not by the narrator (§4.2, §5.4) | The starter worlds carry lore |
| Models select lore in the World Info interceptor (D13), with pins (D19), adoption and suppression (D11) | Retired: lore handling is off, user books follow native World Info, and the engine run delivers only the engine's sections (§6) | The engine's own lore search serves its helpers instead (§5.4) |
| Decline after the prose: other method / remove / accept | Pay another way retries savings → card → checking → cash; Remove and Accept reword the next Outcome note (§9.5) | "Accept" reads as accepting the decline |
| "Not an action?" undoes an interpreted Speed through | Only interpreted action resolutions get the chip (§11) | A timelapse's events are clock output |
| A confirm chip for every confirm-mode proposal | Only proposals that would validate now get Apply (§11) | Apply always failed for unpriced items |
| Prompted steps may use host macros with `commit:false` | Engine placeholders only (§5.2) | Helper calls skip prompt assembly |
| Where a step's output goes was unspecified | §5.2 | Corrections and suggestions stay under the player's control |
| Dynamic values never contain `{{` or `}}` | Every brace, escape sentinel and shorthand is neutralized (§6.3) | Breaking only `{{` pairs left runnable forms |
| A rebased view drops a failed event | Events may be grouped, and a group is kept or dropped whole; packs may add guards (§4.3) | A dropped payment left its history line and the bought item |
| `interpret` maps typed text to the world's actions | The catalog always includes "Let time pass"; a typed duration up to how long the user lets a skip run without asking (a day by default) runs as the turn's timelapse, and an *until* however far off it is (§9.2) | A cap on durations keeps a misread number from skipping days |
| Import fills gaps from tables "marked assumed" | `sources` and `assumed` fields, `FieldDef.table` (§9.7) | The plan named no fields |
| One option per palette target | A second step for two-target and numeric actions (§12) | Two-ref actions had no way to pick their second target |
| An in-memory fold cache meets the 50 ms gate | Persisted fold snapshots (§4.3) | A cold fold at 1k turns took 158 ms |
| The D32 preset lint reads the active preset | It reads a dry run of the open chat (§13) | Spindle has no read API for the active preset setting |
| Action tick of 30 seconds | 1 minute: settings and markers round ticks to whole minutes (§9.2) | |
| A command palette (D12) | Not built; the input bar's palette and the Calendar panel cover Timelapse and Pause (§11) | Not on the critical path |

**Known limits of the engine.** There is no dormant-quest field: a quest that waits for its trigger starts on a bare stage, which the quest log hides until it moves on. Rules fire at slot ticks, so an authored death at 23:05 lands at the next one (Harrowmere's at 06:00 on Saturday), and the body only then stops its routine.

## 21. Open items

| # | Item | Behavior until resolved |
|---|---|---|
| R4 | **The `characters` permission**, which would let the engine see where an outlet inside a character-card field puts a section (§4.2). | It is not requested; the dry-run search shows whether a section reached the prompt, not where. |
| G1–G12 | The upstream proposals (§16). | The extension ships with the caveats documented. |
| — | Not built: command-palette commands, replacing a data table with another region's, a per-proposal edit step in Import from lore, agenda and secret-track objectives in the starter worlds (they carry narrative to-dos only). | Documented in the user guide where a user would look for them. |
| D20 | The license. | None yet; all rights reserved. |

## 22. Accepted residual risks

The ledger is accepted by an executable property suite (§14): a reference fold, a fake host that replays the host's recorded event orders, randomized operation sequences and named invariants. These are the risks the design accepts.

1. If the backend stalls, the guard isn't answered and Lumiverse stops the generation: nothing goes out without the engine's sections.
2. If two generations in one chat overlap, the host's per-chat lock stops the earlier one; a prepared turn goes to the one generation the guard lets through, and a restart starts once (§5.1).
3. The host-wide worker cost remains until G3.
4. Cross-extension metadata clobbering remains until G2.
5. Activation is confirmed, not delivery: the delivery check finds whether a section reached the prompt, not where it landed.
6. Retired with lore handling: the engine no longer judges user entries, so a card entry forced by another interceptor no longer matters to it.
7. A non-head Continue, a Continue on a message without a record, a stopped Continue and a Continue whose prefix the host re-rendered are accepted unread, like any assistant edit: the world stays as simulated until Re-read edit (Retry read for a stopped head Continue), so the text and the state can disagree until then.
8. Detail first opened during a turn's in-flight window (while it streams or during its post pass) shows "pending" until the window closes; writes made then land after the turn.
9. Before the reply, a Continue renders the head's state, since its target is known only at `GENERATION_ENDED` (until G1).
10. Entries made on another branch stay stored but inert; the Keep report lists them, and they apply again only when that branch is active. A Continue made on another branch leaves its text in the message, unsimulated on this one. Deleting a message drops its inert markers (journaled).
11. A foreign extension's metadata write on a reply that is still streaming reads as the reply's save, opening its P4 window early; a player edit to that turn's own user message after it would then be accepted as the host's re-render.
12. Extraction quality is a floor measured on a weak model (§9.4); only time, location and condition changes start `auto`. Learning on the player's own connection and the per-type pin in Settings are the safeguard.
13. Authored lore keeps its macros, as in native World Info; the import review lists them but does not block them.
14. Keys aren't linked to the vehicles they open, so handing over keys with a lend shows its own confirm chip; a future `opens` relation would fold it in. (A narrated give of the thing lent, or of something inside it such as a lent truck's glovebox, is folded into the loan and offers no chip.)

## 23. Outside agents

A user's own agent harness (Claude Code, Codex, Cursor and the like) manages their worlds through the `lumirpengine` CLI and its MCP server (`docs/MCP.md`). Both live in this repository, and neither needs a change to Lumiverse.

**The way in.** The client goes in by the same doors a browser does:
1. It signs in once with the user's name and password and keeps only the session token.
2. It finds the engine's extension id in the extension list.
3. It takes a single-use WebSocket ticket and opens the socket as a widget runtime, so it never takes over the browser's frontend slot.
4. Over that socket it sends `lre.*` requests as `SPINDLE_BACKEND_MSG` frames and reads the `lre.result` and `lre.error` replies by `reqId`.

The host sends no acknowledgements, so the client sets its own timeouts. The token reaches the whole account, and sign-in is rate-limited, so the client reuses the token and doesn't sign in again for each command.

**The contract** (`AGENT_API` 2, `shared/protocol.ts`). `lre.hello` answers with `agentApi`. A client names itself there with `client: {name, version}`, and the user's diagnostics log that an outside client connected. Beyond that, the contract is these requests and one push (level 2 added `lre.state.get`):
- **The working copy's version.** Every `lre.editor` result carries `version`, the version of the world's working copy: the stored draft's or, with none stored, the count the index keeps (`draftVersion`). Each write counts one, and so does each change to the working copy that isn't a write: a discard or a commit records the count before the draft's file goes, and a head moved under no draft (a Studio install) counts one. So a version never names two states of one world, and a client holding one from before a commit or a discard can't write over what came since. A new draft starts one above the count. A stored draft at or below it was dropped (its file couldn't be removed) and reads as gone; one stored before versions existed reads as one above it, and an index without the count counts on from the stored draft's own. A write, a discard or a commit that sends `version` is refused with `conflict` when the working copy is at another, and changes nothing; one that sends none isn't checked.
- **`patch`** applies RFC 6902 operations (`shared/json-patch.ts`) to the whole draft, or to a copy of the head when no draft is stored. It applies all of them or none, and a failed `test` is a `conflict`.
- **`restore {revision}`** makes the draft a copy of one of the world's revisions. Its `base` stays the head, so a commit makes that content head again.
- **`lre.world commit`** takes a `note`, which is trimmed and cut to 200 characters, and a `version`, checked like an editor write's.
- **`templates`** lists the starters `create` takes, with a name and a summary.
- **`chats {worldId?, chatId?}`** lists the user's chats that play a world that still exists: all of them, one world's or one chat. Each comes with the revision it plays now, after any Upgrade, and whether that revision is `behind` the head; a chat whose ledger can't be read (its world lost a revision it needs) comes with `revision: null` and the `reason`. Each listed chat's ledger is opened to read that, and folded once it has upgraded, so a filter keeps the cost to the chats asked about. The host lists chats newest first by offset, so the list shifts while it is read: the pages overlap, and the front is read again until it shows nothing new. `upgrade` answers `pending` when the chat is mid-turn; its marker lands when the turn ends.
- **`lre.draft.changed {worldId, version, stored, base}`** goes to every session of the user after a write, discard, commit or restore of a world's draft, after a lore save, and after a Studio install moves the head.
- **`lre.state.get {chatId}`** answers a world chat's state view, the one a page's snapshot holds, with its activity line. A client's hello names no chat: one that named a chat would open its ledger, push the chat's snapshot and activity to the client's session and ask it again for a prepared reply, and a resync would make the session a page (§5.1). `lre.state.get` is only a read: it makes no page, pushes nothing, asks no one to start a reply, claims nothing and opens no ledger window. The chat tools read the view through it and send the requests Play sends (`lre.fix`, `lre.edit`, `lre.marker`, `lre.chip`, `lre.keep`, `lre.view.open`, `lre.action`, `lre.gm`, `lre.minds`). A turn they post (`lre.action`) is prepared by the engine as any is, but only a page restarts the stopped generation, so its reply starts when a page shows the chat.

**Following the extension.** The client is two bundles: a core (`dist/lumirpengine.js`: the stdio transport, the sign-ins, the connection and the kit loader) and an agent kit (`dist/agent-kit.js`: the tool registry and everything it calls, the guide, the docs, the schema, the starters, the bundled engine and the command line), joined by `KIT_API` (`cli/kit-api.ts`, level in `shared/agents.ts`). `bun run build` gives every bundle one build id (`LRE_BUILD_ID`): the source commit's short SHA, marked `-dirty.<seconds>` when the sources have uncommitted changes, else the time; the ledger's snapshots key on it too. It builds the kit first and defines its sha256 into the backend (`LRE_KIT_SHA256`). An outside client's hello is answered with `build` and `kit {path, sha256, api, core}`, the paths derived from the backend's own module URL: Lumiverse's worker imports `dist/backend.js` by its absolute path in `<data>/extensions/<identifier>/repo/`, in every runtime mode. An MCP server connects at its client's `initialize`, and looks on connect, before a call once its last look is 30 s old, every 60 s while connected (counted from its start), and on `lre.kit.reload {client}`; a look is `lre.agents {op: "kit"}`, which also says whether the session checked in since the extension started (`known`), and an unknown one says hello again. **What it loads.** A followed kit runs with the user's rights, and the server names the file, so the hash alone can't be the guard. The core (`cli/follow.ts`, on the entry's `KitFiles`: Node's fs) loads a kit only when the reported path and its real path (`realpath`, every symlink resolved) are absolute, plain and end `/extensions/<identifier>/repo/dist/agent-kit.js` (either separator); on POSIX, when each directory from `extensions/` down to `dist/` (`lstat`) and the file (`fstat` of the handle it is read through, so the bytes are the checked file's) belong to the user or root and have no group or other write bit, else it refuses with `unsafe`, "*path* can be changed by other users" (on Windows, `uid` is null and owners aren't checked); and when its sha256 matches the reported one, which only catches a file caught half-written or rebuilt since the extension started, not a lying server. It is imported from the checked bytes as a data URL (Bun's module cache ignores a file URL's query), and only when the build differs from its own; then it swaps the tools and sends `notifications/tools/list_changed`. Node 22+ and Bun 1.4 import a data URL of the real kit's size (about 4 MB; `follow-entry.test.ts` runs the entry under the Bun on `PATH` with one), while Bun 1.3 refuses any over about 2 KB (`NameTooLong`, with the whole URL in its message). So under Bun before 1.4 (`runtimeCannotFollow` in `cli/version.ts`, passed to the session by the entry) following is off before it is tried: the server checks in with `runtime`, "run it with Bun 1.4 or newer, or Node 22 or newer (Bun before 1.4 can't load the kit)", never looks, and `--version` notes it. Anything else keeps the tools loaded; `--pin` (`LRE_PIN_KIT`) keeps the bundled kit. The tools' agent API need is the loaded kit's. `LiveError` and `LreError` cross the two bundles by a `Symbol.for` brand, which `instanceof` checks.

**Check-ins and the update notice.** An MCP server's hello carries `client {name, version, serves: "mcp", core, kit, follows, why?, host, harness, listed}`, on connect, after each change of what it runs, and after a `tools/list` it answered with another build's tools than the last. `harness` is its MCP client's `initialize` `clientInfo.name` (null when it gave none); the server connects at `initialize`, so its first check-in names it. `listed` is the kit build whose tools it last answered its client's `tools/list` with (null before the client asked; absent from a core before Reconnect in Lumiverse). The server keeps the stance its last hello sent (`onHello` hands it back), so a list answered while that hello was on its way is still told. A command-line run says `serves: "command"` and is not kept. `Agents` (`backend/agents.ts`) keeps the latest check-in per server in the user's `agents.json` with when it was seen (at most 32). A server is its name, host and harness: Claude Code's and Codex's servers on one machine are two lines, each with its own state, while a restarted server, or another process of the same harness on that machine, is the same one. One with no harness keeps the id it had before harnesses were sent. One with no kit build (from before check-ins) is keyed by its name alone, apart from every server that follows, so neither overwrites the other's state. Stored entries are keyed again by these rules when the file is read, the latest of two that meet kept. **Processes.** `Agents` also keeps each open session (one per server process) with the server it checked in as, its own latest check-in and when it was last heard (a check-in or a look), until `FRONTEND_SESSION_CLOSED`. Those heard lately are written to `agents.json` (`processes`, by session) with every change and every look, so a restart, which an update is, doesn't forget them: on start they are restored, and each counts toward its server's line as it last stood, without being connected or known (its next look says hello again), until its session says hello, closes or goes unheard for `LIVE_MS`. A server's line is its worst process heard from in the last 2 minutes (`LIVE_MS`, two of a process's minute looks), ordered `cannot` < `behind` < `updated`, with that process's check-in and the count (`processes`); with none heard lately (all quiet, or none restored), its stored check-in. So a process that reloaded never makes another that lags read `updated`, and one that went quiet (hung, or asleep) stops counting. A look from a process that had gone quiet pushes the view again; one that ages out shows at the next view pushed or asked for. A server that doesn't follow (pinned, or with no kit to follow) doesn't look, so its processes count only for 2 minutes after each check-in. The build the extension started as is kept in extension storage (`agents/build.json`): another one at start is an update. Each user's next page hello then gets `lre.agents.changed` with the view (the notice; the setup that follows, the extension's core as a command under each runtime of `RUNTIMES`, `bun` first, from `followCommands` in `shared/agents.ts`; each server's state: `updated`, `behind`, `cannot` or `old` for a hello with no kit build) and one `spindle.toast.info`; the notice stays until `dismiss`, and comes back with the next update. A check-in pushes the view to every session of the user, so a line turns as soon as its server reloads. Update now (`update {client}`) sends `lre.kit.reload` to every session of the user, since a restarted extension hasn't heard from its servers again yet; only the servers with that id look. Settings → MCP server reads the view with `get`. **Bun first.** Every place that offers the setup (Settings → MCP server, the can't-follow and from-before lines' copies, a kit-level refusal's text) gives the `bun` command, then the `node` one: Lumiverse runs only on Bun (1.4.2 or newer), so a Bun that follows is always on its machine, where a following server must run, and a machine with only Bun has no npm or npx.

**Servers Lumiverse runs.** The frontend calls none of Lumiverse's own routes (`/api/v1/mcp-servers`, its `reconnect`, its `PUT`): they are Lumiverse's, not Spindle's, and an extension that set the command Lumiverse spawns would run code outside Spindle's sandbox. `boundaries.test.ts` holds the frontend and the packs' UI to Spindle's messages. A server Lumiverse runs itself checks in like any other, under its MCP client's `initialize` `clientInfo.name`, `lumiverse` (`LUMIVERSE_HARNESS`; Lumiverse's `src/services/mcp-client-manager.ts` makes its client as `new Client({ name: "lumiverse", version: "1.0.0" })`). That client lists a server's tools once, when it connects, and handles no `notifications/tools/list_changed`; Spindle can't reconnect a profile (G13). Lumiverse's stdio allowlist (`src/services/mcp-stdio-policy.ts`) runs `node`, `bun`, `deno` and `python`, so it registers the extension's core as `bun <path>/dist/lumirpengine.js mcp` (or `node …`). It passes the server only `PATH` and the variables set there: the credentials file is still found from the user's home, but an `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` must be set there too.

**Reconnect in Lumiverse.** A Lumiverse-run line's view carries `lumiverse {stale, unknown, profiles, reconnect}`. `stale` counts its connected processes that hold another build's tools in their client than they serve (`listed` set and not their `kit`); `unknown` its processes from a core that sends no `listed`. On a line that reloaded, the button shows while `stale` is above 0; with only `unknown` processes, or an old list Spindle shows and no process to ask, the line keeps the manual instruction. On the user's press alone (`reconnect {client}`), the backend sends `lre.kit.reconnect {client}` to each stale process's own session (`frontend.send` with its session id). The core checks again before it acts: its id must be the one named, its harness `lumiverse`, and its `listed` another build than the kit it serves. Then it logs why on stderr, waits until every message fed so far is answered and written (`LineServer.drain`), closes its connection and exits (`serveMcp`'s `exit`, `process.exit(0)` in `cli/main.ts`). Lumiverse's client sees the stdio transport close and connects the profile again about a second later, with its unchanged command (G13): the new process runs the updated core, whose bundled kit is this build's, and Lumiverse lists those tools. The press records the asked sessions, the line's sessions then, the stale profiles Spindle shows (below), and the user's wait (`timeouts.mcpReconnectMs`, Settings → Timeouts → MCP server reconnect, 1 min, `"none"` for no limit, no ceiling). The line is back, and the reconnect dropped, when: with stale profiles found, Spindle's `status` shows each connected and holding this build's list (asked every second while waiting, and on each of the line's check-ins and closes); without, every asked session has closed and at least as many new sessions of the line have checked in with `listed` and `kit` both this build. Past the wait it is `late`: the line says so with the wait and the manual instruction, offers the button again while a process is stale, and still turns back if the server returns. The extension never creates, edits, connects or disconnects a profile.

**Lumiverse's own list (`mcp_servers`).** The manifest asks for `mcp_servers`, a privileged permission an owner or admin grants in Lumiverse's Extensions tab; without it nothing here calls Spindle, Reconnect in Lumiverse still works on the processes' word, and the line says Lumiverse's own list can't be checked and how to grant the permission. With it (`spindle.permissions.has`), a view with a Lumiverse-run line reads the user's profiles (`backend/lumiverse-mcp.ts`, through the port's `mcp`): the list, paged by 100, keeping only each stdio profile's id and name, then each one's `status`. Spindle gives no command or arguments, so a profile runs lumirpengine when the tools Lumiverse's client holds for it include `lre_guide` and `lre_status` (`KIT_MARKS`), which every kit lists, read-only or not; a profile Lumiverse isn't connected to holds no tools and can't be told apart. Whether that list is this build's is its fingerprint (`toolsFingerprint`: `hashJson` of each tool's name, description and input schema, in order, as Lumiverse's `McpDiscoveredTool` keeps them) against this build's two, read-only and full, which `bun run build` takes of the built kit's lists and defines into the backend (`LRE_TOOLS_FULL`, `LRE_TOOLS_READ_ONLY`), as it does the kit's sha256. Every other profile is read only as far as its tool names and dropped: the view carries only the matched profiles' names and whether each is current, and nothing of a profile is stored.

**Concurrency.** Requests are handled concurrently, and two writers that both read the same draft would each write it back without the other's change. So every write of one world waits for the one before it, through one queue per (user, world) in `WorldStore`. That covers editor writes, discards, commits and restores. It also covers the writes that reach a world from outside the editor: lore saves (which pick a free id from the draft inside the queue), job proposals (which the client applies as editor writes), Studio installs, the lore book id kept in the index, and deletion.

A push goes out after the reply to the request that made the change, so the session that made it already holds the new version and has nothing to reload. **Build** names the version it holds on every write, on a discard (the version shown when it asked) and on a commit, and one refused as stale reloads the draft. A push newer than what Build holds reloads it once a burst of pushes stops (`QUIET_MS`), with one toast; an older push, or one from Build's own write, is ignored, and a load already under way finishes first, since it may have read the change already. Build also reloads after a commit, and when its page comes back (the socket reconnects, the browser is back online, or the page is shown again), since it may have missed a push. A reload never resets a form being edited: when the draft's value of what a form edits changed under its edits, Build's bar says *This item changed elsewhere* with **Load theirs** and **Keep mine**, and a save over that change asks first (*Save over the other change*).

**History.** `history.json`, beside the world's index, keeps one note for each time a revision became head: `{revision, at, source, note?}`, oldest first, where the source is `create`, `build`, `agent`, `import` or `studio`. `lre.world get` returns it as `history`; the index and `list` leave it out, so listing worlds stays small however long a world's history grows. A commit from a session that said hello with `client` is `agent`'s, and the dispatcher keeps those sessions in memory. Committing the head as it is adds nothing. A world made before history was kept starts its history at its next head.

**Failures.** A discard or a commit records the count before it removes the draft's file. When the count can't be written, nothing has changed and the request fails. When the file can't be removed, the request fails saying what was done (`… but its file couldn't be removed (…); it no longer reads as the draft`), and the file, now at or below the count, reads as dropped, so it never locks the world out of committing.

**Guards.** A patch is refused, as `bad_request` naming the operation and its path, when it:
- brings in more than 4 MiB of JSON (`PATCH_SIZE_CAP`): the values it adds and replaces and what it copies count, so copies that double the draft stop early;
- would nest the world deeper than 64 levels (`DOC_DEPTH_CAP`), counted where each value lands, moves included (`put` and `set` are held to the same cap for their item or part);
- leaves an item in a world collection under an id that isn't 1 to 200 characters long;
- carries more than 500 operations;
- names a prototype key (`__proto__`, `constructor`, `prototype`) in a path or anywhere in a value;
- changes `/id`, `/format`, `/engineVersion`, `/pins` or `/suppressed`;
- changes a place, grid or route a map draws, or marks anything but a map with `map`. This is the same rule as `put`, checked by comparing the draft before and after.

What a patch leaves must pass the shape checks the Studio seam's `validate` makes, or it is refused as `validation`. After that the document is migrated and its maps compile, as after any collection write. An item a patch or a `put` leaves in a shape the engine can't read at all (its lint, migration or map compile throws on it) is refused as `validation` too, naming the item, before anything is written.

Every editor write that changes a table (`put` or `remove` on `tables`, a `profile`, or a patch) gives that table its content's revision, `hashJson({schema, columns, rows})`, and pins the world to it in `pins.tables`. A removed table loses its pin. A revision the client sends is never used.

A commit is refused as `conflict` when a stored draft began on a head other than the current one ("the world changed since this draft began; reload it"), since committing it would put back what was made head since. Discarding the draft, or restoring a revision onto the head, clears the way.

## 24. Character minds

Simulated perspectives drive the characters, and the mechanics ground them. Each character the story is close to gets a **mind**: one model call made as that character, on a context scoped to what they know, blind to every other mind. It never writes narration or dialogue. It answers with how they read the moment, what they intend and how they come across, and the narrator depicts it. Concrete outcomes (money, moves, time) are still settled after the reply, from what was actually written (§5.1, §9.4). Every answer is recorded as an event (`minds.*`, the `minds` pack), so a fold, a Re-narrate and the background lane never call a model.

### 24.1 The fidelity ladder
- **Levels:** Live, Near, Far, Background. A character's level is recomputed every turn and at every time-skip beat, as a pure function of the state and the turn's texts (the player's latest message and the reply before it; none inside `advance` or at a beat).
- **Focus score:** the sum of the world's weights for each input that holds:
  - **Proximity:** at the POV's very place, else close by (under the same parent place). The ladder keeps to the place itself, so a level, which routine roles read (§8), never moves with the scene's wider reading below.
  - **Engagement:** named or addressed in the latest message or reply. A name counts whole, or by a word of it that singles them out; honorifics never count.
  - **Their relationship to the POV:** regard either way, scaled by its size, and a `knows` relation either way.
  - **Story involvement:** they own a track under way; up to three tracks under way name them in their stages; their role facet values, a value nested under a weighted one counting as it (a villain under antagonist); and they are named by a world rule.
  - **Pending interactions with the POV:** an invitation, an appointment together, an open plan in force (§24.8) or commitment with the POV, up to three.
  - **Tier and recency:** the detail band of their tier, and being seen with the POV lately (all of it right after, nothing a day later).
- **Who decides:** the GM's pin in the chat decides first. Then the author's `never` selector (always Background), which wins over `always` (always Live). Then the score against the world's thresholds, each read as at least the next one down. Live is only for people at hand: someone neither in the POV's scene nor named this turn is Near at most, however high their score, so a Live slot never goes to someone across town while a person in the room has none. "Close by" scores but doesn't count as at hand: it means under the same parent place, which in a town is the whole town. The GM view shows each input's points, any pin and that cap.
- **The POV's scene** (`together`, `engine/presence.ts`): two places are together when they are the same place, or one lies at most two levels inside the other (`LEVELS_APART`: a floor, a suite on it, a room of the suite) with no spread-out place on the way up, so someone the story placed on "the fourth floor" is with the POV in the bath of a suite on it. A place is spread out by travel distance: some route between two places inside it, both its ends within it, takes something other than walking, or a walk of more than ten minutes (`INTERIOR_WALK_MINUTES`). It is spread out too when routes run inside it but short walks don't join all of its parts to one another: travel then joins its parts only by those routes, so a town whose homes and sites are each their own island is spread out though no long route runs inside it. That is a town or a region, and being somewhere in it isn't being with whoever is in one of its buildings. Short walks that join all of a building's rooms never spread it, so a building authored as rooms joined by one-minute routes, or a floor plan's doors, stays one building, and a place with no routes inside, whose parts travel joins on foot, is never spread out. Three levels or more apart isn't together, so everyone a routine puts somewhere in a big building doesn't fill People and Minds while the POV is in one of its rooms. Rooms side by side are never together. The Minds section (§24.3), People and Stance (§6.6), minds' presence (who heard a turn, §24.4, and whether a mind is with the POV, §24.5) and who thinks all read the scene this way, on the state the sections render from.
- **Who thinks this turn** (the user's cadence, §24.6): minds follow the scene and what's in motion.
  - Everyone in the POV's scene thinks every turn, best on the ladder first, up to the Live limit ("none" for no limit); so does a Live character elsewhere while they're involved (below).
  - Someone elsewhere thinks only while they're in something that could surface soon (`involved`): a plan or appointment with the POV within the coming day, an invitation to the POV still open, an agenda of their own under way, the GM's intent for them (§24.9), a place or activity the story holds them to, or a mind of theirs that interrupted the skip the clock stopped on. Idling, living their routine or being named calls no mind.
  - Near characters, and those past the Live limit, come due on their triggers: their first mind, a rise on the ladder, entering the POV's place, being named, a number of turns, or a number of game minutes since their mind was last due. Far characters come due on their first mind, a rise on the ladder, or their period. The calls among them share the refresh limit, the most pressing first. A GM's request for a fresh mind always runs.
  - A mind that comes due but isn't called makes no model call: its level and where they were go on the turn's `minds.round` (`levels`), kept as `passed`. The cadence counts from it as from a mind, and `minds.tier`, which routine roles and skip lives read (§8), reads the level a mind was last due at, called or not, so who thinks never moves anyone's routine. A round recorded without `levels` folds as it did.
  - Background characters follow routine only.

### 24.2 The world's minds config
`world.minds` (optional; a world without it plays with the defaults, `worldMindsOf`):
- **`secrecy`:** `brief` (the default) or `full` (§24.3).
- **`weights`:** points for each input of §24.1, from −100 to 200 (`roles` by classification value).
- **`thresholds`:** the lowest score for `live`, `near` and `far`.
- **`always` and `never`:** selectors, or null.
- **`memory`:** the defaults for §24.4: the witness lines shown, recall's recent and relevant turns and tokens, the notes shown and kept, the open commitments shown, salience's half-life in game days and fade floor, and consolidation (on or off, and its period in game days).

A value out of range or of the wrong kind plays as its default, and lint (`minds.config`) says so. The world JSON Schema describes the part (`$defs/minds`). Build writes it whole as the `minds` part.

### 24.3 What the narrator gets: the Minds section
- **The section:** a section of its own (slot `minds`, outlet `lre_minds`). Every world has it, even one that defines its own sections. It takes this round's minds for the people in the POV's scene (`together`, §24.1) that the POV can see, plus anyone whose mind interrupted a time skip; a mind someone elsewhere thought while involved (§24.1) never shows. A mind from an earlier round isn't the narrator's, nor one from a time skip's beat unless the skip ended on that beat (the clock is still the beat's): the run went past any other. Only the narrator reads it: like Stance (§6.6) it is no fact, so the helpers' fact lines (`factLines`, read by `brief`, `outline`, `choices`, `contradiction`, the effects templates and the step placeholders) leave it out, and no mind reaches a helper or a chip one writes.
- **Its budget:** a token budget of its own (Settings → Minds), apart from the section budget, so minds never crowd out Scene or People. When the section is over its budget, its lines are fitted best on the ladder first (the fidelity order with the player's message): the lowest are left out one by one until it fits. The inspector records how many lines were left out (`cut`), and a section whose best line alone is over the budget is left out whole, as a clipped section is.
- **`full` secrecy:** each mind's monologue, intent, conditional reactions, mood and depiction go to the narrator. The section's note says to depict them, never to state a thought, and never to let one character act on another's mind. Under `full` the narrator may read a character's own secrets in their monologue; that is the author's choice.
- **`brief` secrecy:** only the depiction, written as behaviour anyone present could see. It is screened as Stance's cues are: an unrevealed secret (`findLeaks`) or a name the POV can't see (`namesHidden`) withholds it. The screen runs when the mind is recorded (the event is marked `withheld`, which only the GM view shows; the inspector reads the call as `recorded`), and again when the section is drawn. The monologue stays in the GM view.
- **Stance:** whoever has a line in the Minds section the narrator gets is left out of Stance (`minds.minded`; the engine run's read context gives only the lines that fit). Stance still tells of everyone else with the POV, someone whose line the budget left out included.

### 24.4 Memory
Four layers, each tunable (the world's defaults, then the user's overrides):
- **Witness log:** who saw or was told each notable event. After each reply, `minds.presence` records who heard the player's message: everyone acting at the POV's place when the turn started (the plan's start state, seen by the POV or not), with the turn's notable lines up to the POV leaving them, which reach each of their logs as seen. The lines are an onlooker's (`witnessLines`): only what anyone there could see (people coming and going and what they do, things taken, handed over or lent, someone appearing), screened as player lines are, with the POV named "the player character", never "you"; the POV's own matters (its accounts, needs, what it learns or uncovers, invitations to it) are none of theirs. Whoever the POV is with at the turn's end and didn't hear it gets a second record with no message, so they never recall the turn or remember it: on a turn that takes the POV somewhere else, everyone at the destination (a companion who came along too) with the lines from its arrival; otherwise someone who came in while it played. A `memory` answer's `told` reaches each listener's log as told, a listener being someone who was with them for the turn (who heard it with them, or, unless the POV left them behind, whoever it met), never anyone elsewhere. A told line is for the GM view only: it is another character's memory step's reading of the turn, so it never reaches the listener's mind or memory step (§24.5), and the listener, who was there, has the turn itself. Life-state knowledge keeps its own record (§8).
- **Recall by presence:** the chat turns a character was there for, built here from the presence records, since the host's memory can't filter by who was present. It reads the turns' messages through the host's chat (the player's message and the reply as displayed, hidden JSON blocks left out). It takes the latest `recall.recent`, plus the `recall.relevant` that share the most words with the moment as it is to them, oldest first, within `recall.tokens`: what the POV says and how the last reply ended when they are there to hear it, else only where they are. Their lore is looked up the same way, so nothing the POV says elsewhere chooses what a character remembers or reads. A character never recalls a turn they weren't present for.
- **Model-written notes:** after each reply, the `memory` step runs once for each character who heard the player's message, at the level the user chose for memory (Standard: Live; Full: Live and Near) as it was where the message was said. Someone the POV left behind is told so, and that what the story tells after it left happened without them. It is written from their perspective and based only on what was written: up to three notes with a salience from 1 to 5, the commitments the turn made and resolved, the plans with times the turn states (said aloud, agreed with someone, or scheduled in the story as written; never a guess at what they might do), what they told whom, and their summary when the turn changed it. Its answer format shows the plans as an empty list, with their fields in the line about them, so an example doesn't prime the model to fill it.
- **Commitments:** promises, debts, grudges, favours and plans with someone. They stay until kept, broken or released.
- **Routines aren't memory's:** a lasting change to someone's week the turn states (a new job or shift, a habit, giving one up, a move) is no plan and no note of theirs. The reply's read records it, as a change to their routine with its turn (§8, §9.4), for anyone the reply names, whether or not they heard the turn and with minds on or off; the memory step, which reads a character's own view, never writes one, so nothing of a mind or of a character's own guess reaches their routine.
- **Salience and consolidation:**
  - A note's salience halves every half-life. One under the floor has faded and is no longer shown.
  - Notes past the number kept, and faded ones, are folded into the character's summary by the `consolidate` step, at most once per period per character and for at most two characters a turn.
