  - The state keeps hard bounds whatever the settings say: 64 notes (the least salient go), 48 open and 16 closed commitments, 24 open and 16 closed plans, 48 witness lines and 400 presence records.

### 24.5 A mind's call
- **The context**, built only from what the character knows. A mind reads the story as it stands, never the engine's guesses about it nor anything of another mind; the same context serves the round before the reply, a GM's Run minds now and a time skip's beat.
  - **Always in it, never cut and never subject to a setting:** who they are (their entity's fields, a list of words or ids (`traits`, likes) as a comma list, and when what they're doing ends as the world's day and time in words, only while that end is still ahead and still the activity's own (`activitySpanEnd`), never a bare count of minutes; never a Lumiverse character card: the extension doesn't request `characters`, §3; their agendas; and their own secrets whole, whatever the player has found out: the truths of every track they own (an agenda's included) and of every track a cue of theirs stands for, and the secret lore that is theirs); right after that, the intent the GM set for them (§24.9), so their mind reads it first and works from it: what they mean to do now, in the GM's own words, and how they go about it (`intentLines`); the scene as they perceive it (who they can see, and how they stand toward each); and the occasion (clock, day part, holidays, weather).
  - **Each source after that is the user's to switch on or off and to limit** (Settings → Minds → What a mind reads, `minds.reads`, §24.6). Within its limit a source comes whole, and nothing else cuts it: there is no limit on the context as a whole.
    - **The story as it stands** (on by default): the lore pinned for them; the lore the turn activates; how the story's latest reply ended, when they heard that turn (someone the POV met only at its end, or who came in since, isn't told), whole or its end within the limit; and what the POV just said and did, if they are with the POV. Off, the moment says only that the POV is with them.
    - **The chat as the player sees it** (off by default): Recent chat (`history`), the latest messages before the turn as the player sees them (hidden JSON blocks left out, `hideJsonBlocks`), oldest first, at most `messages` of them and about `tokens` in all, whichever runs out first, the oldest that doesn't fit whole keeping its end (`recentMessages`). With a turn in hand (before its reply, through a time skip, after it for the memory step) that is the chat before the turn's message, which the call reads as the moment; for a GM's run between turns, the chat up to now. A mind that reads how the latest reply ended doesn't get that reply again here: Recent chat gives the messages before it. It tells of turns they weren't there for, so it is off unless the user turns it on. The call's trace counts the messages given (`history`). The memory step, consolidation, the `skip` step's own call and the skip plan (§9.4) read it with the same setting while it's on: the minds steps have no history setting of their own, as they have no lore one, and a minds step card keeps no `recent`.
    - **Their usual routine** (on by default, 400 tokens): their routine blocks in force (the basic needs aside) and the slots of their own place and activity schedules they still follow in this chat, today's under way or still to come first, as habits to weigh, never orders: the block's title says that what is happening now comes first (`usualRoutine`, `packs/castlife/core/usual.ts`). A private block only when it is their own routine's or the chat's change for them; a group's, a kind's or the world's private block is someone else's business. Never the day the planner lays, so nothing the planner filled free time with (meals, friends, invitations) reads as their habit; that stays in their life. With their routine role `off` (§8) they read none. The call's trace counts the lines given (`routine`, never the lines, which can hold a private block), and its notes say how many were left out over the limit and where to raise it.
    - **Their plans** (on by default, 400 tokens, `plans`): their open plans in force (§24.8), soonest first, each with its kind, its words, its time, its place and who else is in it (`planLines`): what the turns stated they will do and what the GM set, so a mind keeps to what it agreed from one turn to the next. A plan everyone in it shares (§24.8) is each one's own, in its words only for the one whose memory recorded it (`by`) or when the GM wrote them: to anyone else in it, it is its kind, time, place and people (`planWords`), since another memory's reading of a turn never reaches a mind; the memory step reads their plans the same way. Never the day the planner lays, which stays in their life. The call's trace counts the plans given, never their words, and says when some were left out over the limit.
    - **Their own memory and life** (off by default): their summary; their notes and what they saw (never what the witness log says someone told them, §24.4); their last mind; recall by presence, told to ignore narration of other people's private thoughts, whole turns within `recall.tokens`, the latest first; and their life (a plain money summary, `economy.summary`; the rest of today as the Cast life planner lays it; their open commitments). Each can carry what the story never set up: the planner fills a free evening with dinners and friends, and a commitment the memory step took from narration that depicted another mind is that mind's, not the story's. Read by default, they turned into intents and invitations the turn knew nothing of.
    - Each limit is in tokens, at 3.2 characters a token (the section estimate's), with no ceiling; `"none"` is no limit. A text (the reply, the message, the summary, the last mind) is cut at a word; lines (memory, routine, plans, life) are kept whole, one that doesn't fit left out; lore is given whole until its limit runs out, the entry it runs out on cut at a word, or left out once not even its title and a few words fit.
  - **Lore read as them:** public entries, `known_by` entries by what they know, and of the secret entries only their own, whether or not the gate holds: the entry's `holders` name them, or a cue of theirs stands for it (the cue mapping Stance reads, §6.6). An entry's `links` say what it is about, so linking them doesn't make it theirs, and the POV uncovering a secret tells no other mind of it. Their own secret entries are in who they are, so they never come twice.
    - **Pinned:** below.
    - **Activated:** the entries the turn names whole, as World Info activates them: by a key, an alias, a title or a lookup-table term (`LoreQuery.exact`), never by links, topics or shared words, ranked as search ranks them. The turn is what the POV says and how the latest reply ended (as displayed) when they heard it, else the name of where they are. An entry pinned and given isn't given again.
  - The moment: a moment the player said nothing in (a time skip's end) also tells them why they think now, and a beat tells its time and reason, with no reply or message.
  - Wherever story text appears (recall, the moment, the memory step's turn), a line says that its "you" is the POV, never the character. Narration speaks to the POV in the second person, and a mind told "You are X" would otherwise take it as itself.
  - **The memory step** reads who they are, their summary, every open commitment by its id and every plan, what they remember (as above, nothing told), the lore pinned to the memory step (below), and the turn as written: the player's message and the reply within the message's and the reply's limits (their switches are a mind's; the memory step always reads the turn). Nothing is cut for room, and it reads no other lore. Only the characters who heard the turn remember it (§24.4).
- **Never in it:** the GM view (but for the intent the GM set for this character), another character's mind or intent (their memory's reading of a turn included), or a turn they weren't present for.
- **The answer:** JSON `{monologue, depiction, intent, reactions[], mood?, interrupt?{reason}}`.
  - Lengths are capped.
  - **No plans.** A mind sees what its character knows and how the latest reply ended, not where the story is going, and asked for plans it filled the gap with an ordinary day the story never set up: errands, meetings, trips and payments that then moved people, money and time skips. So a mind proposes none, and any plans its answer gives anyway are ignored. Plans come from the memory step, which reads the turn as written, and from the GM. A mind's plans an older chat recorded stay in the state as thoughts that drive nothing (§24.8).
  - The memory step's plans resolve to place and people ids and game minutes, and one with no time, or wholly past, is dropped. A time is read strictly: a local `YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM`, a day (its midnight) or an `HH:MM` (the next such time), on a day the calendar has and from 00:00 to 23:59 (`clockOfStamp`); anything else is no time.
  - `interrupt` counts only at a time-skip beat.
  - An answer that isn't JSON gets one repair call. One that says nothing records no mind.
- **Prompts:** the steps are `mind`, `memory`, `skip` and `consolidate`. Each has built-in instructions (`MIND_PROMPT_DEFAULTS`).
  - **Precedence:** the user's own prompt (Settings → Minds, with a reset to the default), then the world's step template for the purpose (`worldMindTemplate`: the first by id with any text, a blank one skipped, which lint and Build read too), then the built-in.
  - **Placeholders:** `{{character}}`, `{{world}}`, `{{pov}}`, `{{place}}` and `{{clock}}` are filled. The time-skip beats' call thinks for no one: its `{{character}}` is empty, `{{place}}` is the player character's place and `{{clock}}` the skip's start.
  - **Answer format:** the engine appends the purpose's answer format (`MIND_ANSWER_FORMATS`), which no template replaces, and then the preset's `RPEngine · <purpose>` block (§6.8).
  - **Lint:** a minds template's signals are ignored (`minds.step_signals`), a blank one is skipped (`minds.step_blank`), and every other template for the purpose but the one used is warned of (`minds.step_duplicate`). The runtime, lint and Build → World → Minds pick the used one alike (`worldMindTemplate`: the first by id, in `localeCompare` order, with any text).
- **Calls:** every mind is its own call, made in parallel on the pre phase's wall clock, apart from its call count. The inspector lists each call (§6.5) with no text: purpose, phase, level, outcome (`recorded` or `failed`), time, tokens, whose prompt it ran, and the character by name. A call for someone the POV can't see or hasn't met isn't listed at all, so the panel never shows that someone unseen is in the room or that an unmet person matters; a history kept by an earlier build, which named them "Someone", reads back without those calls.
- **Pinned lore** (the world's `lorePins` part, `{characters: {id: [pin]}, places: {id: [pin]}, steps: {purpose: [pin]}}`; a pin is `{lore: id}`, `{book: id, entry: id}` or a whole book, `{book: id}`, each with an optional `keep`): what is pinned to the character, then to the mind step (every mind's), then to their place and each place holding it, nearest first (`pinsFor`), is read once per run and given first in the lore block, within the pinned source's limit and nothing smaller; an entry pinned twice, or pinned and in a pinned book, comes once, where it first comes. A pin that doesn't fit is cut at a word, or left out, and the call's trace says so (`pinned[].cut`, `pinned[].left`) and why (`notes`: how many were cut and left out over how many tokens, and where to raise it). A cut keeps the entry's start, or with `keep: "end"` its end (`Title: …last words`, at least the snippet's fewest words), for an entry written oldest first; a whole book's entries keep what its pin keeps. The trace lists the activated entries too (`activated[]`), and neither list names a secret. Pins live on the world, not on a place, because a map-drawn place's Build page is read-only. A pin is world data, so a chat reads a new one once the world is committed and the chat Upgraded; a book's text is read live.
  - A world entry is read as the character reads lore: a secret one only when it is theirs, an AI-written one only once accepted, a `known_by` one by what they know.
  - A book entry is read with `world_books` straight from its book, attached to the chat or not and switched off in it or not; LRE's own engine book is never offered. One anything holds back is gated as a world entry is, by what the interceptor and live lore hold it to (`secrecyParts`, §4.2: its LRE fields, the world entry indexed from it, every secret of its text): its secrecy, gate, audience, holders and Known by links hold for every reader of the pin, the traces and the minds' listing included. The extension doesn't request `characters` or `personas`, so the picker can't tell which books a character card or the persona brings.
  - A whole book is read with `world_books` at every call that reads it: its switched-on entries in the order the host lists them (`world_books.entries.list`: the entries' Order, then id, as the book shows them), so an entry added to it, a Lumibooks chapter say, is read without pinning it. An entry the engine wrote (its own book's sections, the Minds section among them, which holds what minds gave the narrator) is never read through any pin.
  - **A step's pins** (`steps`, any step purpose) are read by every call of that step. A helper's come first in its lore block, before the POV's place's, within its lore tokens and whatever its lore policy. The mind step's go to every mind, after the character's own, and a mind at a time skip's beat (or its end) reads the `skip` step's after those. The memory step, consolidation and the `skip` step's own call read only their step's, as a "Pinned lore" block within the pinned source's limit (their step cards have no lore setting), each entry read as the character for a memory or a consolidation (a secret that isn't theirs stays out) and as no one for the `skip` call.
  - Lint: `pin_shape` (error), `pin_missing`, `pin_target`, `pin_duplicate`, `pin_unread`, `pin_pending`; each path is `lorePins.characters.<id>`, `lorePins.places.<id>` or `lorePins.steps.<purpose>`, and Build → Check opens a character's or place's page for its pins.
  - **Build** (`frontend/build/lore-pins.tsx`): a character's and a place's page each have a Pinned lore section saying what reads its pins, and the world's Pinned lore page a section per step, the minds steps saying who reads theirs and what holds for every step said once at the page's top (`PINS_NOTE`, `KEEP_NOTE`). A pin's `keep` choice sits under its name, not beside it, so a phone's row keeps its title; the note says when to keep the end: an entry written oldest first, such as a running timeline, a log or a journal.

### 24.6 Settings and cost
- **The user's minds settings** (`UserSettings.minds`, Settings → Minds): on or off; the Live limit; the Near triggers and their counts; the Far period; the refresh limit; the levels that get memory; what a mind reads (`reads`); the Minds section's budget; memory overrides; prompt overrides; and a time skip's caps (beats per skip, minds per beat). "No limit" is `"none"`.
- **What a mind reads** (`reads`, §24.5): for each source, `{on, tokens}`; recall's limit is the memory setting `recall.tokens` (the world's, or the user's override; it may be `"none"`), so `reads.recall` is only `{on}`. The defaults:

  | Source | Default | Why |
  |---|---|---|
  | Lore pinned to them and to where they are | on, no limit | the author pinned it for them |
  | Lore the turn activates | on, 1,600 tokens | the turn names it; Standard's former lore tokens for a mind |
  | The latest reply | on, no limit | the story as it stands |
  | What the player says | on, no limit | the story as it stands |
  | Recent chat | off; 8 messages, 3,000 tokens once on | it tells of turns they weren't there for; once on, what `interpret` reads before a typed message |
  | Their usual routine | on, 400 tokens | the world's and the chat's routine and schedules, never the planner's day: habits to weigh, never orders |
  | Their plans | on, 400 tokens | what the turns stated and the GM set for them, never the planner's day: what they agreed to do, kept from turn to turn |
  | Their story so far, notes and what they saw, last thoughts, recall, their own life | off, no limit once on | each can carry the planner's day, or another mind's commitments by way of narration |

  A settings file saved before `reads` keeps the limits it set: its reply allowance (`replyTokens`) is the reply's limit, and under Custom the Character minds step card's lore tokens are both lore sources' (a card with lore off turns them off). Under a preset that card's lore was the preset's, whose cap cut pinned lore, so the sources take their defaults. Its switches take the defaults. The minds steps' cards have no lore of their own: what a mind reads is set here, and the memory, skip and consolidate steps read only what is pinned to them (§24.5), within the pinned source's limit.
- **Model use presets:** a preset sets all of these but what a mind reads, the prompts and memory overrides.
  - **Lean:** minds off.
  - **Standard:** a Live limit of 3, two refreshes a turn, memory for Live.
  - **Full:** no limits, memory for Live and Near.
- **When minds run:** whenever they are on and the purpose's step card isn't switched off. An unsaved card (Lean saves none) runs on the helper model, and Settings → Steps shows it so (`stepConfigOf`), so a change made on it keeps it on; a saved one sets the connection, the effort and the tokens. What a mind reads, its lore included, is set in What a mind reads, never on a card. The `skip` step is the exception: unsaved, it is off, and every preset saves it off (§24.7). The Minds section's own budget (`minds.tokens`) is 1,200, 2,400 or 4,000 tokens by preset, and recall defaults to 4,000 tokens; neither has a ceiling.
- **Cost:** minds' calls aren't counted in §5.3's per-preset tables. A turn makes one call per mind that runs, and after the reply one `memory` call per present character at a chosen level and the occasional `consolidate`.

### 24.7 Time skips
A timelapse turn (§9.2: the palette, a chip or typed time) is a **time skip** while minds are on: before the reply, the skip finds its beats, lets the minds of the people each beat involves think there, and may stop where one of them would interrupt it (`backend/skip.ts`). The background lane and Re-narrate never run one, and minds off leaves a plain timelapse. Nor does one run while a fight holds the clock (§11): the plan refuses that timelapse, so no mind is asked for it. Typed time that states a day and time ends the skip exactly then: "I put in my shifts all week. Skip ahead to Friday at 6 PM." typed on Monday morning skips to Friday 18:00, with the week's beats on the way, unless something stops it first. The skip, its plan, and the minds at its beats and at its end read the lore the engine run read, World Info read live included (§4.2).
- **The state it starts from** is exactly the plan's: the fold before the turn's target, the turn's decisions and its minds round, and the turn's first steps (§5.1). So it reaches each beat in the state the plan will.
- **The beat scan** finds the following without a model, only up to where the skip would end on its own:
  - the POV's appointments;
  - the plans in force (§24.8) of Live and Near characters (as recorded: the GM's pin, else their latest mind's level) that involve the POV, where the planner lays them;
  - from a scratch run of the skip: invitations to the POV, story moments (a track changing stage; a time rule, or one that interrupts or tells a beat), and the POV's money (paydays, bills, statements, declines, at their own instants).

  Beats at the same minute are one.
- **The `skip` step** is off unless the user turns it on. Its prompt is the user's own, else the world's template, else the built-in (§24.5), and the answer format follows it. It is given the span, the beats found and what the people involved plan, and proposes at most five more beats, each a local time inside the span with a reason. Its reasons, like the scan's, are recorded in `minds.skip` for the GM only: no mind and no player surface is given them, since they may come of someone's private intent. Its call joins the inspector's minds calls (§24.5) as the step alone, with no text, no one's name and no level (`level: null`).
- **The caps:**
  - **Beats per skip** (`minds.skip.maxBeats`) keep the weightiest: plans with the POV, then invitations, its appointments, story moments, its money, and the step's last.
  - **Minds per beat** (`maxMindsPerBeat`) keep the people the beat names first, then whoever is with the POV at Live or Near by fidelity at that moment, best score first.

  `"none"` is no cap, and the before-the-reply clock still bounds it.
- **Running it:** one timelapse segment, run beat by beat under one unit cap (only its first slot runs whatever the cap). The cap is checked at a slot's start, as for one piece: a beat inside a slot the cap can't run is never reached, and when the events cap runs out inside such a slot, the run goes back to its start, so a cap stop is always on a slot boundary (§14).
  - At each beat, each of its people thinks in a call of their own, blind to the others, and the reason they are given names only the time.
  - Their `minds.mind` events apply at that moment, so a later beat's minds think on from them.
  - Past the last beat the skip runs to its end, where whoever is with the POV thinks once more, within the Live limit, as the turn's own minds for the Minds section. They are asked as a turn's minds, told the skip has ended, not as a beat's, so nothing they answer can interrupt it.
- **Stops:**
  - **A mind's `interrupt`** stops the skip at its beat. The summary records `stoppedBy: "mind"` and who. The stop chip reads "Timelapse stopped: <name>" (or "someone", for a character the POV doesn't know) and offers Continue to the original target. The Outcome line and the outline say who stepped in; the Minds section tells how. Only the mind that stops it keeps its interrupt: another that would have interrupted at the same beat is recorded without one, so the narrator isn't told they stepped in too.
  - **The before-the-reply clock** (Settings → Model use, at most 105 s) running out stops the skip at the next beat it reaches, the first one too when it ran out before it, so every Continue gets further; whatever that beat's minds finished stays, and no one is asked after it ran out. The summary names no stop, and the Continue timelapse chip offers the time left; the turn never fails.
  - Any other stop (a `stop`-class signal, the unit cap) is the timelapse's own (§9.2). A slot a beat falls inside is still stepped once, at its end, and its stop check reads every signal raised in it, before the beat and after, so a `stop`-class signal ends the skip exactly where the same span run whole ends, and its Continue's scan finds the beats after it.
- **Recording:** the skip's record is every beat it reached, each with the events decided there (none when its minds gave nothing: a failed call, or no one to ask), and where it stopped. The plan replays it: the same segment, cut at the same beats, stops where the run before the reply stopped, in the same state, and applies the same events. The turn's events are therefore an ordinary timelapse record (§4.3), coarsened, holding `minds.skip` (the beats, their times, reasons and sources) and each beat's minds. A re-fold or a Re-narrate reads them, and a Re-run asks again.

### 24.8 The planner
The Cast life planner (§8) lays each character's own plans into their days and lives them; plans already recorded drive it whether minds are on or off, on the background lane too. It lives only an LOD1 agent's plans, from Cast life 3, as it plans only their days: anyone else with a mind (an NPC who isn't an agent, or one below Cast life 3) follows routine only, as a Background character does. Their plans are never laid, so they make no beat and no invitation, and a world process (`castlife.plans`) drops each once its window has passed ("routine only").
- **Plans in force.** A character's plans come from the memory step and the GM. A plan a mind made in an older chat (`source: "mind"`, from before minds stopped proposing them, §24.5) stays in the state, and the GM view shows it, but it drives nothing: no block of their day, no invitation, no payment, no beat, no fidelity point and no "Plan" line in a mind's context. Every reader takes plans from one place (`keptPlans`, behind `minds.plans`), which keeps only those in force (`planInForce`), so none can drift from another.
- **One plan shared by everyone in it.** A plan with others in it (`with`), from the memory step or the GM, is theirs too (`sharesPlan`, `MindPlan.shared`): a payment or a loan, which goes one way, is the planner's alone. It is one recorded plan, under one id that who is in it and when it starts give it (`sharedPlanId`), whoever's memory writes it; applying it puts a copy from each one's side (`with`: everyone else in it) in the list of each person in it the world has but the player character, whose plans are the player's (its invitation asks it along, below). So a replay folds every copy from the one record. A memory's plan keeps whose memory recorded it (`by`): its words are that memory's reading of the turn, which no one else's mind reads (§24.5); a GM edit that rewrites them makes them the GM's, which everyone in it reads. Each holder lives their copy as the planner lives anyone's (`livesPlans`, so someone who isn't an agent, or a Background character, drops it as "routine only") and settles it on their own; each list keeps its bounds (24 open, 16 closed). After a reply, each memory of the turn records only the plans its character doesn't already hold by id (`unheldPlans`), applied one after another, so a plan two people made together is recorded by the first memory and the second writes no copy. A GM edit of a shared plan from anyone's page reaches everyone in it, each keeping how they settled it; someone taken out of it loses it, someone added gets it, and a delete takes it from them all. A plan recorded before plans were shared has no `shared` and folds as it did.
- **Laying a plan.** A character's plans in force (`minds.plans`) become blocks of their day: the open ones, and the settled ones for the times before they were settled. So a plan that's done keeps the day it had, with the needs it met (and the Cast timeline that shows it), and no other plan re-lays into a past it was cut from.
  - **Its window** starts no sooner than the moment after the plan was made: a plan for "now", or one already under way when the memory step or the GM records it, starts a minute after, when the next turn or timelapse can live it.
  - **A tight window** (at most twice what its kind takes) is the plan's own time.
  - **A wider window** gets its kind's length (an errand 45 minutes, a meeting 90, a visit 120, work 240, rest 60, leisure 90, other 60). It goes at the first time in the window, within its kind's hours (errands 9 to 18, meetings 10 to 22, visits 10 to 21), that trips, appointments, the stronger plans and, for a plan that doesn't take its time from routine, the routine leave free. The search covers the window's first 14 days.
- **Precedence:**
  - A plan takes its time from routine (work, school) when it is strong (priority 4 or 5), or, for a Live or Near character, ordinary (3) at a set time.
  - A Far character's routine bends only for strong plans, and a Background character follows routine only. A plan is laid and lived whatever their routine role (§8): an informative routine (Live, Near and Far by default) moves no one, so only the story's plans and the GM take them anywhere.
  - A plan that must happen (5) overrides appointments too.
  - Plans cut each other by priority, then age, and a trip overrides everything.

  Laying is a pure function of the plans and the authored commitments, never of the clock or of where anyone stands. Every plan that can bear on the span asked about is laid with it (those whose windows reach it, and those whose windows reach theirs), so a plan lands at the same time however often, and from whatever timelapse segment, its day is planned.
- **Living it:**
  - A plan's block starts and ends at its own minute, between slot steps, as every block of their day starts at its own (§8): an agent process's moments (`ProcessDef.instants`), applied among the schedule edges in a turn and a timelapse, for an agent stepped through the slot before. Catch-up steps by slot.
  - At the start the character goes to the plan's place (home for rest), and holds its place and activity for the block's span, as a trip does: a routine's schedule edge inside it (work starting at 08:00 under an errand until 08:30) doesn't take them back.
  - After the end they take up whatever follows, going home when that names no place.
- **Settling it:** at each slot step a `minds.plan` event marks a plan done once every block laid for it has passed. It marks one dropped once its window has passed with nothing laid: "no time for it" (routine, appointments, a stronger plan or a trip left no room), or "routine only" for a Background character. A plan cut away entirely is dropped, never done. Each records its own time, so a late catch-up settles it alike.
- **Money plans:** a `pay` or `lend` plan is no block of anyone's day; the economy carries it out (§25.5). Its amount (`cents`; the memory step writes `amount` in the world's major units) goes to the first person the plan is with, and a plan with no positive amount or no one to hand it to is left out. It's paid as a one-off at its window's start, never before the minute after it was made, from the planner's own money as their preferences choose, at the planner's Money level: in the money step's time order from Money 2, and from a world process (`economy.plans`) below it, so no plan is left open for want of an engine. It's done once paid, and dropped with the decline's reason when the money isn't there. Its journal lines say only "Payment" or "Loan", never the plan's words. The POV's plans are the player's, so none of theirs is carried out.
- **What the player sees:** a plan's block shows its kind ("Errand", "Meeting someone", "A visit", "Work", "Rest", "Time out", "Busy") and "their own plan", never the plan's words, which are the character's private intent. The GM view shows the plan whole (§24.9).
- **Occasions** shape a day only through its routine's blocks (§8): a block's `days` say whether it's on (weekdays, days off, holidays and observances, their own birthday or a close friend's at regard 30 and up, seasons from Weather 1, fair or foul weather from Weather 2), and a block `with` its company happens at the first of them's home (a friend's birthday at the friend's). The `birthday` field is `MM-DD`, or `YYYY-MM-DD` with the year they were born. 29 February falls on the 28th in a common year. A birthday that isn't a date of the world's calendar (`02-30`, `13-05`, or `1994-02-29`, a common year) is no occasion, and lint flags it. No occasion brings an option on its own any more: the occasion-shaped template of earlier builds only folds what they recorded (§8).
- **Needs** (Cast life 4) follow the plan. An NPC's need is met at the end of the latest block that meets it, or now while they're in it, and the routine's times stand in before the day has met it. Options weigh needs as the day so far leaves them, so a plan through the lunch hour leaves them hungry for dinner. A need the world has off weighs nothing, and the world's rate sets how fast each grows (§8).
- **Invitations** (Cast life 4) come only on a ground: a plan with the POV (the memory step's or the GM's), or a routine block that asks its company along with the POV among it (while `routineSettings.invitations` reaches the player). One is asked at most 6 hours before the meeting, never before the meeting's day, and a plan's at the earliest the moment after it was made, so one for "now" is asked in time; and only once (it is keyed by the plan or the block and day; a shared plan's by the plan alone, so whoever in it asks first asks for them all), with its ground recorded (`origin`). None comes from a Live or Near character, from someone with the POV, or from someone the chat set to make none; none goes to a POV that doesn't act, is held away, or is already where they'd meet; and none passes between people who don't know each other or where the host regards the POV badly. Its text never names a place the POV can't see. Nothing asks the POV along for company's sake alone: the formula invitations of earlier builds are gone.
- **Accepting books it.** Accept invitation (`castlife.book_invitation`, after the regard it appraises) adds an appointment to the POV's `appointments`, so its day has it: at the meeting's time, for as long as the host's own block for it lasts (an hour when it can't be found), at the meeting's place when the POV can see it (else at no place, so its day never takes it somewhere it doesn't know of), labelled with the host's name alone, never a plan's words, `with` everyone in the plan that asked (the host alone for a routine's), and keyed by the invitation (`invitation`), so accepting it again books nothing more. Its day lives it as any appointment: a Timelapse following its routine takes it there, and its people count as involved with it (§24.1).
- **Stance** leaves out whoever the Minds section tells of (§6.6, §24.3).

### 24.9 The GM view
- **What it shows:** per character, their level, score and each input's points, the pins, whether a fresh mind is asked for, the intent set for them, their latest mind (monologue, depiction, intent, reactions, mood, interrupt, and whether its depiction was withheld), their plans, notes (with their salience now, and whether faded), commitments, witness log and summary. A plan a mind made in an older chat is listed as a thought from their mind, which drives nothing (§24.8), and isn't counted among the open ones.
- **The controls,** each an `lre.edit` operation, so one `fix` entry with its Undo:
  - pin a level, or unpin (`mind_pin`);
  - ask for a fresh mind next turn (`mind_refresh`);
  - add, edit or delete a note, commitment or plan (`mind_memory`; a value names only the fields that change). A plan with others in it is shared, under its shared id when added; an edit or a delete of a shared plan reaches everyone in it (above).
  - set their **intent** (`mind_intent`, `{entity, text?, approach?}`, recorded as `minds.intent` with the intent whole): what they mean to do now, in the GM's own words (at most 600 characters), and how they go about it: `reactive` (they answer the scene and what is asked of them, and start nothing of their own), `proactive` (they pursue their own aims unprompted) or null (their mind decides). Each part is optional and a part left out is kept; `""` and null empty one, and with both empty the intent is cleared. Only that character's own mind reads it, first after who they are (§24.5), so what it intends, and through it the story, follows; no other mind, no other step, no narrator's section and no player surface is given it. It stays in the chat until changed or cleared, so a Re-narrate and a Re-run keep it as they keep a pin. Proactivity is a choice of its own because a mind told only to read the moment answers it: without it, the GM could steer what a character wants but not whether they act on it unasked.
  - **Run minds now** (`lre.minds`, `{op: "run", who?}`): one character, those chosen, or (nobody named) everyone Live and Near, asked from the chat so far as the turn would, with the same lore (World Info read live, each entry as secret as its text is, §4.2) and pins, four at a time. The moment is the waiting player message if there is one, else how the latest reply ended (reason `BETWEEN_TURNS`), and the reason recorded is `gm`. It opens no `minds.round`, so the narrator's next Minds section has these minds only if the cadence runs them again. Everyone Live and Near is a button that says how many minds it runs and asks before it starts, since each is a model call spent at once.
  - The run's minds are one `fix` entry with its Undo; while a reply is being written the edit is held and lands when it finishes (`pending`). Progress, each mind's outcome with why it failed in words (`helperJson`'s failure reasons), its time, tokens and pinned lore, go to the requesting session as `lre.minds.update`; `{op: "cancel"}` stops it and records nothing, and `{op: "status"}` reads the latest run. One run per chat at a time; it stops at the user's **Minds run on demand** limit (`timeouts.mindsRunMs`, 10 minutes by default, or none).
- **Player surfaces:** never any of it. Every minds event words nothing for a player's line, so no Journal note, chip, Cast or other panel tells a mind. The inspector's traces carry no text, list only people the POV can see and has met, and never say a depiction was kept from the narrator.

### 24.10 Determinism
- **Events:** every model answer is recorded as an event: `minds.round` and `minds.mind` before the plan; `minds.presence`, `minds.memory` and `minds.consolidated` with the reply's read. Each apply reads only its own data, the minds slice and the clock, never an entity's fields, so a coarsened record (L21) folds the same.
- **Swipes:** a Re-narrate copies its source's pre-reply events, so it reuses the pre phase's minds. A Re-run asks again. A Re-read edit writes the memory again from the edited text.
- **No model calls:** the background lane (Playtest, Check world) runs no model and records no mind. Plans already recorded still drive the planner (§24.8).
- **Replay:** a replay, a restart or the other fold gives the same minds state. The property suite generates minds turns, replies that record presence and memory, and GM minds controls, and checks them on every view.

## 25. Money foundation

The economy pack's core (`src/packs/economy/core/`) is one double-entry ledger with an outside-economy counterparty (`economy.external:<id>`), and everything money does is built on it. The work module (§26) and the holdings module (§27) register through the `FinanceRegistry` it hands them (`module.ts`; the list is `modules.ts`): fields on its kinds, flow sources, amount providers, net-worth sources, sale hooks, view sections and summary lines. No module registers a kind or a second ledger, and none adds a top-level world collection: money is entity kinds (account, card, flow, trust, business, job, holding, property, invoice), the person fields `pay_prefs`, `filing_status` and `local_tax`, the place fields `atm` and `branch`, and `packs.economy.config` (`products`, `features`, `assets`, and module keys such as `taxes`).

### 25.1 Parties, owners and roles

A party is a person, a business, a trust or a household. An account, a holding, a property or a business lists `owners: [{party, role, share?}]`. The role grants the rights: `owner` and `joint` spend, manage and count it; `authorized_user` spends; `custodian` and `trustee` manage it for a `beneficiary`, who counts it. Every "your own things" read in the engine goes through `heldBy`, so held accounts and cards count as a character's own. The first owner opens the account. A joint account opens once, by `economy.open`, whichever owner's money materializes first. Shares split what a shared account is worth among its owners.

**Managing through a business or a trust.** Whoever manages a business or a trust, by a managing role on it (`owner`, `joint`, `custodian` or `trustee`), manages every account it holds a managing role on, by that role: its standing payments, its autopay and its cards, as they would an account of their own (`managesAccount`; the `manages(who, thing)` formula reads an account or a party). The same role runs the business itself: hiring, invoicing and payouts (§26.2). A draw is narrower (`may_draw`): only an owner or a joint owner takes one, and never from a corporation, which pays its owners by salary or dividends; each business in a view says whether its reader may (`mayDraw`). They spend from the business's accounts for themselves only by a role of their own on them (an authorized user's card), and those accounts count toward their worth only through their share of the business. An employee holds no role on the business and manages none of it. A role that only spends (`authorized_user`) or only counts (`beneficiary`) manages nothing.

**The fiduciary rule.** A manager runs money they only manage for whom it is, never for themselves, as a trustee or a custodian must. That money is an account of the business or trust they hold no spending role on, or the business or trust itself paying as a party, whatever their role on it: a party's money is never its manager's own, though every managing role on it carries the spending right. It reaches none of their own interests (`interestsOf`): them, their household and its members, a party any of those counts the worth of (a business they own, and so may draw from; a trust they benefit from) and in turn any party those count the worth of (a business their business owns), four holders deep as whom the money is for is followed, or an account whose worth one of those counts. Nor does it reach an outside payee typed as any of them: a name whose key (`payeeKey`, the key an outside payee is kept by) is their id's or their name's, or their household's ("Jordan Ellis" or " POV " for the POV), which the hub refuses by the same keys. A payment among whom the money itself is for, the manager aside, is the money's own (`whoseMoney`): the café's savings topping up its operating account, a trust paying its beneficiary though she's the trustee's daughter. Nor does it go into an account they may spend by a role of their own (an authorized user's card, a custodian's or a trustee's), as they could move it on from there, unless the money is wholly theirs (below), or every holder of the account's worth is someone the money is for: one it ends with, a trust's beneficiary or a business's owner, the manager aside. A business the money passes through on its way to them isn't one of them, any more than the business paying is: where the café belongs to a company, its custodian pays the café's money into that company's till, which he holds a card on, no more than into the café's own till. A share of the money isn't the whole of it, and such an account would be a way round the rest of its people: a trustee who holds a tenth of the trust as a beneficiary pays the rest of it into no account he may spend, a tenth owner running a corporation pays its money into no account he holds a card on, and the café's half-owner, holding a card on its till, can't sweep its savings into it, while its outright owner can. Its custodian, who holds none of it, can't either; a trustee pays the trust's money into no account he's custodian of for anyone else; and a parent who is trustee of his daughter's trust and custodian of her account pays her into it, as money paid to its own people is theirs, whoever holds the card or the custodianship. What a custodian may do with that account is the custodian's role's, and no part of this rule. A payee named as someone in the world is judged by where the payment lands as well as by who they are: the account it names where that's theirs, else their deposit account (`payeeAccount`), so paying the café is paying into its till, and paying Rosa is paying into her account. So a standing payment from such money, or one falling back on it, can't pay them; a business they run for others can't be billed by one that is theirs or whose operating account they may spend, nor pay such an invoice by their hand, its payment judged by the operating account it lands in; without a share of its worth they put neither themselves nor their household on its payroll, nor raise their pay there, while with one a salary is theirs to take, as a draw is; and unless it's wholly theirs they pay no one else there into an account they may spend, the worker's deposit account or one the worker's pay splits into, unless it's the money's own people's (`payrollProblem`, §26.1). Money wholly the manager's own, where nobody else holds any of its worth (a business they own outright), keeps no one else's interest, so it pays anything else of theirs: the owner of two companies outright bills and pays between them. What they take out of it for themselves still goes by a draw or a salary, so the books and the taxes see it: it pays neither them, their household nor its members. A credit account's autopay draws only on its debtor's own money (a holder whose role counts it): an account they may spend from or whose worth is theirs, so the shop's loan pays from the shop's money, their own card or mortgage from their own, and a custodial card from the custodial savings. A fallback is always the payer's own, as the money step draws on it (§25.5). Each account in a view says, by these rules, whether the party spends from it by a role of their own (`spends`), where its autopay may draw from (`autopayFrom`) and what may back a standing payment from it (`fallbacks`). The view says too, for each payer they run standing payments from and only manage, which of the ids the rest of the view already carries, and of their own interests they can see (their household and its members among them, which nothing else in it carries with households off), it may not pay (`paysFor`), so the list names no one they can't see, and a payee typed by an id it doesn't name is the backend's to refuse; the keys a typed payee is theirs by (`ownKeys`); and on each business whom they may neither hire nor raise there (`selfDealing`), so the hub refuses as the actions do.

### 25.2 Products and classes

`product` names a world product (`packs.economy.config.products`) or a class. Classes are cash, checking, business checking, savings, money market, CD, trust, escrow, credit card, charge card, line of credit, loan, mortgage, brokerage and retirement. A product gives terms (APR or APY, fees, grace days, minimum rule, limits, hold days, term); the finance table's class defaults fill whatever it leaves out. Rules read class traits (`CLASS_TRAITS`: asset, liability, pays, revolving, installment, locked, …), never a class name. A locked class (retirement, CD) can't be chosen to pay, moved from or drawn on by a flow; its own module may post from it.

### 25.3 Cards

A card is an entity on an account, in a holder's hands. It carries a network (debit, credit or charge), a state (active, frozen, lost or cancelled), daily purchase and cash limits, and optionally the gear item that is the physical card. Freezing and reporting a card lost are the holder's actions. A replacement is a new card on the same account, with a new gear card where gear runs; the old card is cancelled, and its gear card, if the holder still has it, stays with them as a card that no longer works. A purchase by card posts at once, pending until it settles a day later where holds run.

### 25.4 One statement cycle

Every credit product runs through one cycle (`cycle.ts`). Revolving accounts close a statement on their statement day: interest falls only on a balance carried past a grace period, and the first statement is always in grace, so an opening balance isn't charged at once. Interest follows the average daily balance where that feature runs, or the month's balance otherwise. Each statement also posts any annual fee and rewards, and sets the minimum due (a percentage plus interest, with a floor). Cash drawn on a card, at a machine or moved into an account, is a cash advance: its fee posts at once (a percentage, with a minimum), and the statement charges it the product's `cash_advance_apr_pct` for the days since it was drawn, with no grace; once carried, it runs at the card's APR with the rest of the balance.

On the due day, autopay pays the minimum, the statement balance, the full balance or a fixed amount, capped at what's owed. If less than the minimum arrives, the account moves through the delinquency stages: late, 30, 60, 90, 120, collections and charged off. A late fee posts, never more than the minimum. At 60 days the penalty APR starts, at 90 the account freezes, and from 120 it goes to collections and then charged off. Each stage is a credit-report entry. Six on-time payments in a row lift the penalty APR.

Installment accounts bill an amortized payment on their due day. `months_left` counts down, the last payment takes everything left, and the loan is paid off at its term. Deposit accounts earn interest and pay any maintenance fee on the 1st.

### 25.5 Flows

Every scheduled movement of money is a flow: authored flow entities, and flows the foundation and its modules derive. The foundation's own are card and loan autopay, living costs, vehicle premiums and characters' plans in force to pay or lend (`plan:<planner>:<plan>`, a one-off each, §24.8). A vehicle's premium is derived only where no insurance flow (kind `premium` or category `insurance`) insures it: one linked to the vehicle, or one its owner pays that's linked to nothing. The modules' are payroll, owner's draws and invoices.

A flow has:
- a payer and a payee;
- an amount: fixed, from a formula, a table, a percentage, what's due, or an amount provider's;
- a schedule (`every`, `interval`, `days`, `weekdays`, `months`, `except`, an `at` time, `once`, start, end and count), with a roll to a business day;
- splits;
- a link to what it belongs to;
- a shortfall policy for when the payer can't cover it: skip, partial (as much as one payment can take), defer (added to the next occurrence), retry (after a number of days, a set number of times; one retry carries what later bounces with it), fallback (the payer's own: itself, or another account one of its holders may spend from), or overdraft. Only `overdraft` pays into an account's overdraft room, with its policy's fee or linked transfer; every other policy acts when the money itself isn't there.

The money process (`economy.money`) runs every occurrence, statement and due check in a slot at its own instant, in time order. It works on a scratch state, so two payments due the same morning see each other. A payment to someone in the world lands in their deposit account; any other payee is an outside counterparty.

A flow entity's payer changes it with `flow_update`, which can rename it (`economy.flow_renamed`, 1 to 80 characters) and, with `clear` (a comma list of at, start, end, count, roll, retry_days, retries, fallback and category), take those limits off it. The GM's `economy.flow_edit` renames and clears anyone's.

### 25.6 Journal and holds

Every posting writes a journal line on each account it touches: memo, kind, category, its other side, card, flow, and status (pending or posted). A posting records both its sides, who the money came from (`payer`) and who it went to (`counterparty`): the parties or outside payees its poster names, else the holder of the account its biggest line on that side is on. Each line names the side opposite it, so money out names whoever was paid and money in whoever paid it: the shop's takings name its walk-in customers, an invoice paid to it its customer, and the customer's own line the shop. A line whose other side is the party reading it, or the one party whose money its account is (money between their own accounts, a business's too), names no one, and so does an opening or an adjustment; on an account two hold, what one of them pays in reads by their name to the other. Events from before postings recorded their payer named only the payee: money in from them names whoever in the world paid it where the posting shows them, and a line kept naming its own holder reads as no one. The journal keeps the last 40 lines per account, monthly totals for 13 months, the last 6 statements and the notes. A hold makes part of a balance unavailable until its time: an authorization, or a deposit's hold days at a cash machine. Release is the GM's control. What can be spent is the balance, less holds, plus overdraft room.

### 25.7 Credit

Each person has a credit report: inquiries, accounts opened, on-time and late payments, collections, charge-offs, closures and payoffs. A score is computed monthly from payment history, utilisation, account age, new credit and mix (`credit.ts`), and the report keeps its history. Applying for credit makes a hard inquiry; the score then sets the APR tier and the limit, or a decline that says why. At every level a lender extends no more than the score earns: a card's, charge card's or line's limit is the finance tables' starter, typical or good-credit limit for the tier; a loan is at most what secures it is worth, else the personal-loan range's top at a prime score or better, its typical amount below, so asking for more is declined. A product's own `limit` caps either. Below the level where credit runs there's no inquiry or score-based decline, and the APR is the product's. Modules apply through `api.applyCredit`, which returns a decline as a `declined` rejection.

### 25.8 Payment preferences

A person's `pay_prefs` lists ways to pay, in order: cash, debit, credit, charge, checking, savings, or a named card or account. Each tier can set `min`, `max`, `categories` and `except`. A payment takes the first tier that fits and can cover it, then cash, debit, credit or a charge card, whichever covers it first; savings, a money market or a trust account pays only when a tier or the payer names it, and a bill's way (`account`) tries checking, credit, charge and then savings. Cash needs a till, an in-person sale where the payer is. A method or account the player names ("with my card") goes before the preferences.

### 25.9 Cash, machines and branches

Where cash needs a machine (the `atm` feature, at the top level), a withdrawal takes one of two routes. At a branch of the account's own bank (a place's `branch`) it needs no card and has no limit. At a cash machine (a place's `atm`, found at the person's place or a place it is inside) it takes an active card on the account. The machine's per-withdrawal limit and the card's daily cash limit both apply. Another bank's machine charges its surcharge plus the account's out-of-network fee, and cash from a credit account is a cash advance, with its fee. A cash deposit is available at once at a branch; at a machine that takes deposits, the account's hold days apply. Below the top level, cash moves freely between the wallet and the accounts.

The machine rule holds only in a world that places at least one `atm` or `branch`. A world that places none, such as a migrated older world or one that keeps banking offstage, moves cash freely at every level; otherwise the top level would leave cash out of reach.

### 25.10 Overdraft

A deposit account's `overdraft` policy decides a payment the balance can't cover. `decline` refuses it. `fee` pays it up to `overdraft_limit` and charges the product's overdraft fee. `linked` draws the gap from `overdraft_link`. A flow from an open account that doesn't have the money bounces with the returned-payment fee, where fees run; one from a closed or frozen account is declined without it.

### 25.11 Households and views

A household is a party its members belong to. Its accounts, the joint accounts every member holds, and its shared flows (paid by it, or split among its members) make its view: who pays what share of the month. `FinanceView` is one party's money at their own level: overview, accounts, cards, flows, jobs, businesses, investments, properties, credit and household. The POV's view is `economy.money`; every party's, POV first, is the GM's `economy.people_money`; and any one party's is `economy.finance`. Player views show "someone" for anyone or anything the POV can't see (`seenBy`: someone or something it can't see, or an account held only by such), and so do the money's reasons for refusing an action (`shownLabel`), which reach the narrator and the decline chip, and where a job's pay is routed.

A view names whoever is outside the world as a person would (`outsideName`), on a flow's two sides and a journal line's other party alike. The foundation's and the modules' own payees, kept by key, read in words: "Federal tax", "State tax", "Local tax", "Property insurance", "Property upkeep", "Insurer" for a vehicle's premium and "Shops" for living costs. A regional tax names its region from the regions table ("Sales tax (Michigan)", "Property tax (Michigan)"). A bank reads by its name ("Lakeshore Credit Union", or "Bank" where it has none), and so does a cash machine's bank. An outside employer reads as the place or the name its job gives ("The Rusty Anchor"), a managed property's tenants as "Outside tenant", a property's outside buyer or seller as "Buyer" or "Seller", the closing a purchase's mortgage pays out to as "Closing", and a holding's market as "Market". Any other outside name, an author's or a player's, reads as written. Whatever such a name says that's in the world reads as the viewer may see it: someone they can't see as "someone", as they would by id; a place they can't see as the plain word ("Employer", "Bank", "Cash machine"; "someone" for a name that's only the place); a story track never. A flow whose stored sides name anyone or anywhere the viewer can't see goes out without its spec, and a job's spec goes out without such an employer or workplace; a job's employer reads the same way on its row (§26.2). Nor does such a flow's own id go out, unless the viewer acts on the flow by it (it's theirs to change, or a lease of a property they own a share of, which they may end): its id can name whom it's for (`pay:<job>`, an author's `bill_jess_household`), so it goes as a stand-in (`opaqueId`, a digest of it), which its upcoming lines carry too. An upcoming line gives the account paying it only where the viewer can see that account. What a flow belongs to (`link`) reads by its name, but a job is named for its worker ("Danny's job at Alder Automotive"): one whose worker the viewer can't see reads by its title and employer ("Mechanic at Alder Automotive"), under the stand-in its row on its employer's page carries.

Each flow goes out once for the whole view, as the viewing party knows it: its row, its coming-up lines and alerts, the household's section and the modules' references to it (a late invoice's alert, a business's invoice) all send the same id, stand-in or not, and give the same controls, while the view reads its run by its own id. A stand-in is a digest anyone can compute from a guessed id, as the world file is readable in Build anyway: it keeps spoilers out of play, not out of a determined reader's reach.

Labels and memos read as the viewer may see too. A derived flow's label is built from names ("Lift loan autopay"), and a posting's memo is written once for every side of it, so on the player's views a flow's label, on its row and in the stored flow its edit form opens on (`spec`), its coming-up lines and the memo of a journal line or a hold read each name of someone or something the POV can't see as that: "someone", an account by its class where the text begins with it ("Loan autopay") and "an account" within, "a card", "a property", "somewhere" for a place and "something" else. Names are read as the engine reads text for them (`namesAny`, §7): a person by any word of their name, so "Rosa's share of the rent" reads "Someone's share of the rent", and anything else by its whole name. A name something they can see also has gives nothing away, and stays, and so does a word of any name they can see, whatever it names (a person, a place, a business, an account, an item), or of a job's title: with Delgado's Deli on the map, "Delgado" stays though Rosa Delgado is hidden, with Rusty's Garage among their payees "Rusty" stays though Rusty Malone is, and "Cash for the week" keeps the name of their wallet though Cash Holloway is hidden. A flow's label, a job's name and an invoice's memo are the writing this reads, not names, so none of their words counts as seen.

### 25.12 Realism gating

The Money level sets a baseline.

| Level | What runs |
| --- | --- |
| 1 | A wealth band, following a notional net worth. |
| 2 | Cash plus one account. |
| 3 | Every account, cards, flows, payment preferences, households and living costs. |
| 4 | Statements, interest, fees, delinquency, credit, cash machines, holds, limits and overdrafts. |

Each feature in `FEATURE_DEFS` has a floor (the lowest level it can run at) and the level where it turns on by itself. A world's Custom toggles (`packs.economy.config.features`) switch a feature on anywhere from its floor up, or off. The views follow them: statements, holds, credit, the household, standing payments, payment preferences and cards show where their feature runs, and full detail (terms' fees and penalty rates) from Money 4. So do the actions that change them: setting up and changing standing payments (`flows`), payment preferences (`payment_prefs`) and a card's state (`cards`) each require their feature to run for the actor (`money_feature`), not a Money level, so a switch that shows them below Money 3 opens their controls too. Where standing payments don't run for the party, no section of their view offers a flow as theirs to change (`editable`): the household's shared costs still show where households run, but not to change. One the switches leave off is declined with the world's own reason, that it doesn't keep track of that, as one a Money level gates says the world doesn't simulate it in enough detail. With household shares off, a flow's shares don't split it and its payer pays it whole. Flows take the payer's level when the payer is in the world, else the payee's. Demotion stops processes and narrows views; balances stay live, and the trial balance is 0 at every level mix.

### 25.13 Minds

`economy.summary` is a character's own money in plain second-person lines, for their mind: cash and the bank, what they owe and when it's due, what comes in and goes out in the next two weeks, the modules' lines (their pay, their business), and what's pressing. It also returns the band and roughly what they could spend today. It counts only accounts whose worth is theirs, so an account they may only spend from (an authorized user's, a custodian's) and its bills aren't in it; it never says anything about anyone else's money.

### 25.14 Older worlds

`migrateWorld` reads older revisions through `migrateMoney` (`src/engine/money-migrate.ts`). It converts:
- an account's `owner` and `type` to its owners list and product, and `remaining_months` to `months_left`;
- an autopay sentence ("minimum from acct_x") to a mode and the account it pays from;
- a deposit account's `apr_pct` to its `apy_pct`;
- a party's `bills` to flow entities (a weekly bill's weekday given by name or number), dropping a lender's bill that the loan's own autopay already pays (one naming the loan, or the lender's for the loan's payment), and making a vehicle insurance bill an insurance flow that insures every vehicle its payer owns, as the old bill did;
- a person's occupation and payroll fields to a job: an hourly rate first, then `salary_cents`, then `annual_income_cents`, else the occupation's pay;
- an economy config that names no region to the POV's `region`, so sales tax at its places runs at the rate its people's income tax does;
- a brokerage's units of a symbol to a holding of that asset (`<account>:<symbol>`, the id the holdings module's trades add to), with the symbol added to the world's assets.

Only a world with the economy pack and the old shapes is migrated, so a migrated world has nothing left to migrate, and a world without money gets no jobs or flows. The starter templates are written in the foundation's vocabulary (`data-src/templates/README.md`).

## 26. Work and business

The work module (`packs/economy/core/work.ts`, with `employment.ts`, `business.ts`, `books.ts` and `taxes.ts`) runs on the money foundation (§25). Every payment it makes is a flow or a posting the foundation checks and journals, and every number it keeps lives in the economy pack's slice, written by its own events inside the posting's unit (§4.3 groups). So both folds keep it alike, a rebased view that drops a payment drops its bookkeeping too, and nothing in it calls a model.

### 26.1 Jobs and pay

**A job** is a `job` entity: one worker's employment.
- **The employer** is either a business in the world, which pays from its operating account (§26.2), or an outside employer by name, which pays from the outside economy. An employer named by a place, as worlds wrote it before businesses were parties, is the business trading there.
- **The pay basis** is one of: `salary` (a year), `hourly`, `per_shift`, `commission`, `tips` or `stipend` (a period). A rate the job doesn't set comes from its occupation row:
  - salary: the typical earnings;
  - hourly: the median wage, or for a tipped occupation the tipped cash wage;
  - per shift: eight hours at the median.

  A tipped occupation's tips are the rest of its median, by the hour, whatever rate the job sets: a raise, or a hire at a stated wage, keeps them.

**The work a job counts.** A job counts only the days from its `start` to its `end` (the last day worked), and inside its `season` (`MM-DD` to `MM-DD`, which may wrap the new year) when it has one. Hourly and per-shift pay count hours, from one of three sources (`hours`):
- **`scheduled`:** the worker's commitments at the job's workplace, as castlife gives them (`castlife.commitments`). That covers their routine there, after every cut a trip or a plan of their own makes. A shift is a day with work in it.
- **`attended`:** the player's own timesheet (`economy.timesheet`). When a slot completes, each work block of the player's that ended in it counts if they turned up: they are at the workplace, or arrived there or left it after the block began. Blocks from before the chat began count as attended.
- **`contract`:** the job's hours a week over the days it counts, thinned to its weeks a year unless a season already says which weeks. A day the worker is away on a trip (its noon is inside one) isn't counted.

With no source set, a worker who has a routine at the job is paid for it: the player as attended, anyone else as scheduled. A worker with no routine there is paid the contract hours.

**The pay schedule** (`pay_every`):
- `weekly` and `biweekly`: anchored per job, counted from its `pay_anchor`, else the first payday weekday (`pay_weekday`, Friday by default) on or after its start;
- `semimonthly`: on the chosen days (`pay_days`, the 15th and the last day by default);
- `monthly`: on any one day (the last by default);
- `custom`: a flow schedule, flattened (`pay_unit`, `pay_interval`, `pay_weekdays`, `pay_days`, `pay_months`, `pay_except`);
- `per_job`: each evening at 21:00, for that day's work.

A payday that falls on a weekend or a day-off holiday rolls to the business day before it (`pay_roll`: `before`, `after` or `none`).

**A paycheck** pays for the work since the last one, up to the start of the day it is paid; a gig's runs up to its payout. Before any paycheck is paid, the work counts from the start of the period the chat's first payday closes.
- **Salary and a stipend** pay each scheduled period the paycheck closes, each prorated by the days the job counted in it. A seasonal salary divides over the periods its season reaches.
- **Commission** is the job's percentage of the sales credited to it since the last paycheck: sales at the business while the worker is at the workplace, and walk-in takings on days they worked, split evenly among those who qualify.
- **Tips** are the job's tips an hour times its hours.
- **Deductions** are fixed amounts or percentages of gross, never more than is left. A pre-tax deduction comes off income-tax wages; a retirement contribution is still FICA wages, a health premium isn't. Each goes to an account of the worker's, or to an outside payee. A business employer adds its match (`match_pct`) to the worker's account.
- **Withholding** follows §26.3, and every tax it takes comes out of what the deductions leave: what doesn't fit comes off federal income tax first, then the local and state taxes, then Medicare and Social Security. The employer's own share of FICA is paid whole. So a paycheck is never refused for its withholding.
- **The net** is routed across the worker's accounts (`splits`): fixed amounts first, then percentages of the rest; what's left goes to their deposit account.

The paycheck is one posting: the gross, plus the match and, at the top level, the employer's share of FICA, out of the employer's account; the net, deductions and taxes to where they go. Its `economy.payslip` rides with it. The payslip moves the job's record on (what it has paid through; the timesheet and the commission sales start again), adds to the worker's year of pay, and books the cost to a business employer. The payday note goes with it too: a signal for the player, a private note for anyone else (§9.3).

**Payroll as flows.** Each job derives one flow (`pay:<job>`, source `job`) from the employer to the worker, on the job's cycle, from its anchor to the scheduled day of the first payday after its last day. Its occurrences are counted by their scheduled day, so a last payday that rolls back before the job's end is still paid.
- It runs at its payer's level: a business's own, or for an outside employer, the worker's (§25).
- Its shortfall is `skip`. Each paycheck covers everything since the last one paid, so a payday the employer can't cover, the first included, is paid with the next.
- A paycheck with nothing earned (a period on leave, out of season, or with no shifts) posts nothing but still moves the record on, so that time is never paid later.

**Hire, fire and raise** are actions for the business's owner and GM edits:
- **Hire** (`job_hire`) spawns a job. It starts today unless given a date, takes what it doesn't set from the occupation, and joins the business's own pay cycle, so its payroll runs on the same day.
- **End a job** (`job_end`, also the worker's own way to quit) sets its last day to today. The next payday pays through it.
- **Raise** (`job_raise`) sets the rate the next paycheck pays.

A manager without a share of the business's worth (a custodian, a trustee) hires neither themselves nor their household, and raises neither's pay: they'd pay themselves from money they run for others (§25.1). With a share, a salary is theirs to take, as a draw is. And unless the business is wholly theirs, they hire or raise no one else whose pay would land in an account they may spend by a role of their own, the worker's deposit account or one a split of the job pays, unless every holder of its worth is someone the business's money is for (§25.1).

A GM spawns a job and sets its fields, or sets its `status`, `end` or `rate`. Payroll reads only the fields, so an edit changes payroll exactly as the action would.

**The old payroll.** The person fields (`hourly_cents`, `pay_frequency`, `deposit_to` and the rest) and the 09:00 payroll process are gone, and the world migration turns those fields into jobs (§25). An old chat's payroll left only plain postings, history lines and signals, so it still folds as recorded. The first paycheck after an Upgrade covers only from the day after the last scheduled payday, so it never pays a period twice.

### 26.2 Businesses

A business is a party (§25) with owners and shares, its own accounts and its books. **Its operating account** is where takings land and payroll and payouts come from: its `operating_account` when that is one of its own, else its best deposit account.

**Sales.** A purchase at a place a business trades at (its `places`, else where it stands; the most closely enclosing one wins) pays that business, through the foundation's sale hook:
- the price goes to the operating account, with the sales tax the business collects on top (§26.3);
- the tax is owed until it is remitted;
- the cost of the goods, the share its `margin_pct` doesn't keep, is owed to its suppliers;
- its commission staff at the workplace are credited.

A business doesn't sell to itself. One with no accounts is paid as any party without them is (§25).

**Walk-in takings.** A business with `daily_sales` takes that much, give or take its `sales_spread_pct`, from customers outside the world. The takings come at closing (17:00) on each of its `trading_days` (Monday to Friday by default), and never on a day-off holiday. Each day's amount is a triangular draw from the occurrence's own seeded generator (§25), and its customers pay sales tax at the business's level. These are its sales when no one in the world shops there.

**Payables** are settled monthly by derived flows: the suppliers on the 10th, the sales tax collected on the 20th, both rolled to the next business day. The sales tax goes to its region's authority (`tax:sales:<region>`). Each flow pays the whole balance owed, so one the business can't cover is paid in full with the next.

**Invoices** are `invoice` entities.
- **Issuing one** (`invoice_issue`) names the customer (someone in the world, or an outside customer by name), the amount, what it's for, and the days to pay: the business's `invoice_days`, else 30.
- **Payment is a flow** from the customer, paid as a bill (from an account, never wallet cash). It falls due on the due date and again every week after, until the invoice is paid in full. An outside customer pays on the due date; one in the world pays once their money covers it. An outside customer pays from money nobody in the world had, so what a business bills outside customers in a calendar month is at most the world's cap on unlisted prices (`unpricedCap`, §9.5); someone in the world pays from their own money, unbounded.
- **Late.** An invoice still unpaid when its due day ends goes `late` and takes the business's `late_fee_pct` of the amount, and the fee again every 30 days it stays unpaid. The customer and the business's owners are told.
- **Paying it now** (`invoice_pay`) is the customer's own action, or its manager's for a business customer. **Voiding it** (`invoice_void`) is the owner's. A business someone runs for others can't be billed by one that is theirs or whose operating account they may spend, nor pay such an invoice by their hand: its payment is judged by the operating account it lands in (§25.1).

**Expenses** are flows the owners set up from the business's accounts, which they manage by their role on the business (§25.1): rent, utilities, loan payments. They reach the books through the journal.

**Owners are paid** in two ways:
- **Draws.** An owner or joint owner takes a draw (`owner_draw`) from the operating account. A corporation has none: it pays its owners by salary or dividends.
- **Distributions** (`distribute_profit`) share out an amount among the owners by their share of its worth. With no amount given, the business pays its `distribution_pct` of each month's profit since its last distribution, less what distributions already paid of that month; each payout records the months it paid (`months`), so no month's profit is paid out twice. A distribution policy (`distribution_pct` with `distribution_every`: month, quarter or year) does the same as a flow on the 10th after each period closes.

Each owner's part goes where the holdings module routes their stake (`economy.stake_route`, else their deposit account). A reinvested part stays in the business. Every part is recorded on the stake (`economy.stake_paid`, §27): a pass-through business's as profit, which the owner's estimated tax covers; a C corporation's as a dividend, which their investment tax does.

**The books** are kept by calendar month, the last 25 of them:
- **Revenue** is sales, invoices paid to it, and other money in from the journal (less the takings of sales already booked).
- **Expenses** are the cost of goods, payroll with the employer's costs, invoices it paid, business income tax, and every other outflow from its accounts by journal category.

Owner draws, distributions, transfers between its own accounts and the sales tax it passes through count as neither, and nor do balance-sheet moves: money it borrows (`loan`), and investments and property it buys or sells (`investment`, `property`), which change what it holds, not what it makes. Its receivables are its unpaid invoices. Its payables are the invoices billed to it, the sales tax it holds and what it owes its suppliers. The holdings module values a stake from them (`economy.business_books`, `economy.operating_account`).

**On the dashboards**, a party sees itself when it is a business, and each business it holds a role on:
- its books for the year, and for each month since its books or accounts first recorded anything (the chat's first month for a business it starts with, else the month it began), the last 12 at most;
- its last 20 sales;
- its invoices;
- its staff, with their pay;
- what it owes for taxes and to its suppliers, with when each is next paid;
- from Money 3, the accounts it holds that the party manages through it (§25.1) but holds no role on, so they aren't among the party's own (its loan, say), each as the party's own accounts show. An account of it the viewer can't see isn't on its page at all, not even by id.

A party's own late invoices, owed or owed to it, are alerts. The modules' sections of a view are told who reads it: the player's surfaces name anyone the player can't see only as "a customer", "an employee" or "an owner", and leave out their ids (an owner line's party, an invoice's customer or business, an employee's worker, where their pay goes, and their job's worker and splits); a hidden employee's job goes under a stand-in id (§25.11), as its own can name them. No action takes a stand-in, so the player can't raise or let go an employee they can't see: they can't act on someone they don't know. The GM's views name everyone (§7). A character's money summary for their mind says how a business of theirs is doing this month, and what they owe on an invoice.

### 26.3 Taxes

**Withholding** (feature `withholding`, from Money 3):
- **Flat below the top level:** the job's occupation's federal-plus-FICA rate (`withholding_rate_pct`; the worker's own occupation when the job names none) and the region's state rate (with its local rate for someone who pays it, `local_tax`), on gross pay less pre-tax deductions.
- **Brackets at the top level** (feature `tax_brackets`, Money 4). The period's taxable pay is annualized, the standard deduction for the worker's `filing_status` comes off, and the rest goes through the federal brackets of the finance tables and back to one period. Social Security is 6.2 % up to the year's wage base (counting the year's FICA wages so far). Medicare is 1.45 %, with the additional 0.9 % past $200,000. The state and local rates follow.

A contractor (`contractor`, by default the occupation's `self_employed`) has nothing withheld. A student working for their own school pays no FICA.

**Sales tax** (feature `sales_tax`) applies only at the top Money level (4), or under Custom with its toggle on. It runs at the payer's level: below it, a price is as listed, with no tax line. A business below it neither collects nor remits: a taxed customer's tax goes straight to the authority, and its walk-in takings, taxed at its own level, carry none.
